# BRC-207: 1Color Wallet Module

Ty Everett (ty@projectbabbage.com), David Case (dcase@opl.dev)

Contributors: John Calhoun, Bridget Doran, Darren Kellenschwiler.

## Abstract

This specification defines the canonical `color` wallet module for fungible
assets under [1Color (BRC-162)](../tokens/0162.md), including the
[BRC-161](../tokens/0161.md) encoding of the same token model. It combines
counterparty-derived P2PKH custody, per-asset baskets, overlay-verified admission,
amount-aware spending consent, input selection, change, issuance, authority
operations, burns and composable multi-asset swaps. It supports arbitrary foreign
scripts and both complete and partially signed transactions through the existing
[BRC-100](./0100.md) interface.

The 1Color name and its exclusive conformance use follow BRC-162. Implementers
may use independent product branding; all `color` protocol, basket, label, topic,
lookup and capability identifiers below remain unchanged.

MUST, MUST NOT, SHOULD and MAY express requirements for conforming implementations.
A wallet without this module MUST reject its reserved identifiers. Module support
does not reduce the mandatory core wallet interface or authorize an operation.

## Asset identity, amounts and custody

An `assetId` is the canonical BRC-162 API token ID: a lowercase display-order
transaction ID, an underscore, and a decimal deploy uint32 output index without leading
zeroes, for example `<64 hex characters>_0`. Its binary prefix representation,
including the legacy nonzero-index form, is exactly BRC-162's. The wallet MUST
identify assets by this issuance outpoint, never by symbol, icon, decimals or a
caller label. BRC-161 JSON and BRC-162 binary outputs with the same ID represent
the same asset; BRC-162's binary-prefix precedence applies.

Amounts MUST be processed as exact integers in the domain specified by the
applicable token encoding. BRC-162 value outputs carry 1 through `2^64 - 1` units;
zero designates authority, not an empty value balance. JSON quantities in this
module's metadata MUST be decimal strings without leading zeroes. Sums MUST use
exact arithmetic that cannot overflow when adding multiple maximum-sized inputs.
Deploy `dec` affects display only; there is no conversion between token units and
satoshis, and no floating-point accounting.

Wallet custody requires an admissible BRC-162 prefix, with its optional payload,
followed by an ordinary P2PKH remainder controlled by the module-derived wallet
key. BRC-161 equivalents MAY be received and spent in the same managed baskets;
the wallet MUST recognize and validate both encodings. New wallet-generated
issuance, transfer and change outputs MUST use BRC-162. Arbitrary valid scripts
remain supported for foreign inputs and destinations; the custody restriction
only determines which outputs the wallet can automatically manage and sign.

## Namespace and counterparty derivation

The logical derivation identifier is:

```text
p color <assetId | ISSUE> <derivationPrefix> <derivationSuffix>
```

The prefix distinguishes a payment or issuance and the suffix distinguishes each
output, as in [BRC-29](../payments/0029.md). Exact remittance strings MUST be
preserved, even when they contain base64. They MUST be nonempty strict UTF-8,
without whitespace, and SHOULD be freshly generated. An application origin,
transaction, amount, basket or permission grant MUST NOT silently alter keys.

For interoperability with [BRC-43](../key-derivation/0043.md)'s lowercase
alphanumeric protocol grammar, the wire representation is defined as follows:

- `assetTag` is the asset ID's 64 lowercase txid hex characters, `v`, and its
  canonical decimal output index, or the literal `issue` for `ISSUE`.
- `prefixTag` and `suffixTag` are lowercase hex encodings of the **UTF-8 bytes of
  the exact remittance strings**, not hex encodings of decoded base64 data.
- `protocolID` is `[2, "p color " + assetTag + " " + prefixTag + " " + suffixTag]`.
- `keyID` is the fixed string `color`.
- The BRC-42 invoice is `2-<complete protocol name>-color`.

The complete protocol name MUST satisfy BRC-43's 280-character bound. Invalid or
oversized identifiers MUST fail before key derivation; truncation, case folding
of remittance strings and alternate wire aliases are prohibited. For example,
`ISSUE`, prefix `AQ==`, suffix `Ag==` encodes as protocol name
`p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d`. The logical form above is not a second wire
spelling. This encoding introduces no exception to BRC-43 normalization.

Derivation follows BRC-42's same sender/recipient counterparty relationship used
by the TypeScript Wallet Toolbox's [BRC-29 script template](https://github.com/bsv-blockchain/wallet-toolbox/blob/master/src/utility/ScriptTemplateBRC29.ts):
the sender derives the recipient's child public key using the recipient identity,
and the recipient derives its child private key using the sender identity.
`getPublicKey.forSelf` MUST select the intended owner's child. `self`, `anyone`
and compressed public-key counterparties retain their BRC-100 meanings. Receipt
metadata MUST represent `anyone` by the compressed public key for private key 1.
Ordinary and privileged root selection retain their meanings and authorization;
public derivation from an ordinary identity cannot reproduce a privileged key.

A deploy output uses `ISSUE` because its own transaction ID does not yet exist.
That derivation remains its spend key after issuance: finalizing the asset ID
MUST NOT rederive the deploy's key. Subsequent outputs use the known asset ID.
A wallet MUST store the exact original tuple for every managed output, including
legacy-encoding inputs and authority outputs.

### Baskets and dispatch

The logical baskets are `p color <assetId>` and
`p color <assetId>-AUTHORITY`. Their BRC-100-compatible wire names are:

```text
p color <assetTag>
p color <assetTag> authority
```

There is one value basket and one authority basket per asset, never a global
Colored Tokens balance basket. `issue` is not a stored basket. After a deploy transaction
has its fixed, fully signed transaction ID, the wallet MUST assign its derived asset basket,
or authority basket when its amount is zero. Provisional issuance records MUST
remain pending until that ID is final. Authority output counts and capabilities
MUST NOT be presented as fungible value amounts.

The `color` scheme is defined in [BRC-98](./0098.md) protocol,
[BRC-99](./0099.md) basket, and [BRC-111](./0111.md) action-label namespaces.
Labels carry the action plan below; they do not change derivation. The wallet
MUST dispatch these identifiers to trusted module code before generic permission
defaults. Application code cannot install its own handler or bypass custody by
renaming baskets, deleting metadata, choosing an originator, or obtaining a
generic signing grant. Reads and organizational operations use module-aware
permissions; relinquishing tracking does not release protected keys.

`getPublicKey` and `verifySignature` retain their normal public semantics.
`createSignature` accepts only the transaction request below. Encryption,
decryption, HMAC and linkage operations in this protocol MUST be rejected.
Protected keys and root linkages MUST NOT be exposed through auxiliary methods.
BRC-29 satoshi keys remain separate and are signed only by the normal wallet
transaction path.

### Asset discovery and balance queries

The module MUST support these read-only `listOutputs` views:

| Basket selector | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `p color assets` | Inventory across this root's per-asset value and authority baskets and the pending records defined below. |
| `p color balance <assetTag>` | Available token units in the asset's value basket. |
| `p color balance <assetTag> authority` | Number of available authority outputs in the asset's authority basket. |

These selectors are queries, not storage baskets. They MUST NOT be accepted as
output destinations, internalization baskets or relinquishment targets. Coins
remain in their per-asset baskets. The `assets` view follows the aggregate-query
pattern used by [BTMS asset discovery](https://github.com/bsv-blockchain/ts-stack/blob/main/packages/wallet/btms/src/BTMS.ts),
without requiring a shared storage basket or application-maintained asset list.

#### Inventory

`p color assets` returns ordinary BRC-100 output records for actual outpoints,
not fabricated asset-summary UTXOs. `totalOutputs` counts matching visible output
records, not distinct assets or token units. With `include: "locking scripts"`,
applications can decode BRC-161/162 scripts, resolve deploy IDs from their
outpoints, and deduplicate by canonical asset ID. Authority-only assets MUST
appear; zero authority amount is not a reason to discard them. Identity and
quantities come from validated records and scripts, not application tags or
symbols. There is no one-satoshi filter.

The inventory includes admitted, unspent managed value and authority outputs,
including reserved outputs. It also includes wallet-created, fully signed
outputs with fixed transaction IDs that the wallet has verified it controls but
has not yet credited because consensus finality, submission or overlay admission
is pending. These
pending records remain prospective, outside spendable balances. Unsigned
provisional IDs, unverified incoming messages, spent outputs, foreign custody
scripts and legacy burn records MUST NOT be exposed as held value or authority.
A fully signed pending transaction that consumes a listed source leaves that source
reserved until completion or conflict is established; source and prospective
change MUST NOT both count toward available balance.

For this explicitly defined module inventory view, BRC-100 `spendable` is a
boolean: true only for locally available, admitted, unreserved outputs under
the wallet's accepted state policy; false for reserved or pending records. When
`includeTags:true`, every record MUST carry exactly one wallet-authored state tag:
`color_state_available`, `color_state_reserved`, or `color_state_pending`.
Applications cannot assign or overwrite these state tags. A `spendable:false`
record is discovery information, never permission to spend. Normal authorization
still refreshes source and overlay checks. Inventory is a wallet-state snapshot,
not a promise that an outpoint will remain unspent.

The view MUST honor ordinary tag filters, `include` options, `limit`, `offset`
and their bounds. Filtered state tags are evaluated against the authoritative
state. Records are ordered by canonical txid and then numeric output index,
ascending for nonnegative offsets and descending for negative offsets under
BRC-100's offset convention. Each call's count and page use one coherent
snapshot. Separate calls need not share a snapshot; callers refreshing a
changing inventory must reconcile outpoints rather than count repeated records
as additional value. Implementations MUST report an explicit error instead of
silently truncating a requested result at a private scan limit.

Access requires module-aware read authorization for the inventory scope. Only
records covered by that authorization may contribute to either the page or its
count; neither may leak other holdings. Restricted authorized views are scoped
inventories, not claims about the wallet's complete holdings. `seekPermission`
retains its ordinary meaning. Discovery neither grants signing permission nor
exposes inventory through `getVersion`. Permission UI and grant organization
remain wallet-specific.

#### Exact balances

The balance selectors adopt [BRC-112](./0112.md), including its exact module
balance representation. A value query sums token units, never carrier satoshis;
an authority query counts capabilities, never their zero-valued token fields.
The target asset and role are determined by the selector. Only admitted,
unspent, unreserved outputs available under the wallet's accepted state policy
contribute. Pending receipts and reserved inputs are excluded. The same ownership,
read-permission and tag-filter rules apply as to the corresponding basket.
An existing, accessible basket with no matching available outputs returns zero;
a missing or inaccessible basket uses the normal BRC-100 error behavior.

Responses contain `outputs:[]`, no `BEEF`, and the exact aggregate in
`totalOutputs` as canonical decimal text, including `"0"` for zero. BRC-112's
P-basket string encoding applies to the API, JSON and binary substrates. `limit`
and `offset` do not affect the sum. Each value output remains uint64, but the sum
has no uint64 ceiling. Ordinary inventory counts retain their numeric format.

An unsupported codec or validator, exceeded resource limit, or inability to
establish the requested balance MUST produce an explicit error. No rounding,
wrapping, saturation or partial sum is permitted. `balance p color ...` is
prohibited; `p color balance assets` is invalid because amounts of different
assets must not be combined.

## Overlay spendability contract

For each asset, the topic and lookup service are deterministically:

```text
tm_color_<assetId>
ls_color_<assetId>
```

These names use the canonical underscore asset ID, not the key-derivation
`assetTag`. They cover both token encodings, value and authority. For **every**
input, whether caller-supplied or wallet-selected, the wallet MUST authenticate
the source output, apply BRC-162's binary-prefix precedence, determine its token
asset and role or bare-satoshi status, and derive any topic and lookup service
it needs. The caller supplies no input-topic hint. An application label, basket,
description or other annotation cannot conceal a token, designate another topic,
or select a service URL. A source that cannot be resolved and authenticated fails;
the wallet MUST NOT assume it is bare satoshis.

Before handling an asset, the wallet MUST have a user-accepted configuration for
which providers and evidence it trusts for that asset and how it establishes a
sufficiently current answer. It may use owner-configured or owner-initialized
[BRC-88](../overlays/0088.md) SLAP trackers to discover providers. Discovery
alone does not make every advertised host a required or trusted provider. The
wallet MUST authenticate the providers and response provenance required by its
configuration and apply that configuration's freshness, availability and
disagreement rules before releasing authorization or accepting custody. If it
cannot establish a sufficient answer under those rules, it MUST fail closed.
A historical proof or cached permission does not replace the required current
check. No caller-supplied endpoint or declaration is a trust anchor.

This BRC specifies neither one provider set nor a universal quorum, timeout,
chain-tip tolerance, facilitator, [BRC-101](../overlays/0101.md)-style
negotiation, or asset-network participation authorization scheme. Those choices
belong to the wallet, asset issuer, configured trackers, application, users and
their wider system. Wallets can consequently disagree about admission or
spendability while following this BRC in good faith. An issuer's and
counterparties' conventions for an asset may be necessary for a transaction to
be accepted by its intended recipients. A wallet MUST account for that
interoperability risk in its own authorization and submission policy; a response
from a provider trusted by one wallet is not automatically sufficient for
another wallet.

A conforming service MUST support this [BRC-24](../overlays/0024.md) query:

```json
{
  "service": "ls_color_<assetId>",
  "query": {
    "type": "color-spendability",
    "version": 1,
    "assetId": "<assetId>",
    "topic": "tm_color_<assetId>",
    "outpoints": [
      "<txid>.<vout>"
    ]
  }
}
```

Query outpoints use canonical [BRC-36](../outpoints/0036.md) dot form. The response
MUST be BRC-24 `output-list`, containing exactly the queried outputs that are
currently admitted and unspent in that asset's topic. Each item MUST include its
`outputIndex` and BEEF sufficient to authenticate its source output. An empty
list is a valid negative answer. Any missing queried token outpoint fails its
spendability check; unrelated, duplicate or conflicting returned outputs are
invalid responses. The wallet MUST reconstruct each returned outpoint and match
its bytes to the supplied source transaction, asset ID, role, amount and script.

Services MUST additionally support the same query shape with
`type: "color-admission"`. This returns exactly the queried outputs that were
validly admitted to the topic, including outputs now spent. It provides historical
membership for receipt validation, not current spendability. Missing historical
outputs fail unless the wallet independently establishes their admitted lineage
through a complete BRC-176 proof and verifies the received output's current
membership. Both queries use the same source-byte matching and response rules.

These queries are a minimum interoperability profile, not a restriction on
additional service queries, return-value forms, facilitator capabilities or
token-specific functions at higher layers. Services MUST apply BRC-161/162
admission and authority rules; script prefix presence alone does not prove
membership. A successful response is a claim about that provider's current
service state under the wallet's trust policy, not a miner-enforced token rule,
a proof that every other provider agrees, or a future guarantee that an output
remains unspent. Pending actions require fresh validation on completion.

### Deployment discovery

The shared topic `tm_color` MUST be available before any particular issuance.
Its role is deployment discovery, not per-asset spendability. A provider serving
it MUST authenticate the complete deployment transaction and chain evidence and
validate the BRC-161/162 deploy position, encoding and amount/authority role. It
admits qualifying deploy outputs; it MUST NOT accept an arbitrary asset ID or
caller metadata as a deployment. Its admission does not admit subsequent token
transfers or authenticate other assets in the same transaction.

After its signed bytes and transaction ID are fixed, a wallet-created deploy MUST
be submitted to `tm_color` during normal processing. Configured providers can
observe that deployment and seed `tm_color_<assetId>` and `ls_color_<assetId>`
from the validated deploy and its canonical issuance outpoint. Each resulting
service MUST apply the ordinary per-asset rules and advertise through the
wallet's configured discovery system. No per-asset provider is required to exist
before the deployment transaction is constructed or signed.

The wallet MUST then discover an accepted provider for the derived service and
obtain the actual per-asset admission/spendability result before crediting its
deploy output. A discovery-topic acknowledgement alone is insufficient. If no
accepted provider is available or asset admission fails, retain the exact signed
transaction and prospective basket as pending, report the failure, and retry
discovery/submission idempotently. Do not invent a new issuance or rederive the
ISSUE key to work around that failure. Existing assets consumed or created by
the deployment transaction still require their own checks and submissions.

This bootstrap path applies to wallet-created deployments; incoming
`internalizeAction` still requires existing per-asset admission and MUST NOT
silently submit to either topic. Partial or consensus-non-final deployments
remain pending as specified below.

## Transaction evidence and admission

The caller MUST provide the complete transaction context, authenticated source
outputs and required BEEF/SPV evidence. A bare preimage,
digest, amount declaration or list of hashes is insufficient. Txid-only entries
may be resolved from authoritative wallet knowledge; unresolved sources fail.
[BRC-176](../tokens/0176.md) packets MAY supply token-lineage evidence but MUST
be validated under that profile rather than stripped as ordinary Atomic BEEF.
They do not replace current overlay spendability. Neither `trustSelf` nor
`knownTxids` waives token admission or currentness checks.

The wallet MUST validate all source output identities, scripts, satoshi amounts,
proofs and token roles; reject duplicate inputs and conflicting representations;
and, before releasing a spend signature, check every token input against its
asset overlay's current spendability. For a completed receipt, consumed inputs
instead require authenticated historical admission or valid BRC-176 lineage;
checking them as currently unspent would incorrectly reject ordinary receipts. Foreign inputs contribute
amounts only after this authentication and topical-membership check. The wallet
MUST evaluate the resulting transaction per issuance ID under BRC-161/162:

- A deploy is admitted only at the position and under the rules of its encoding.
- Apply the encoding-specific BRC-161/162 balance and authority rules exactly,
  aggregating by the same issuance ID across encodings. Without authority,
  all non-mint value and explicit-burn output amounts together require value
  input coverage; any uncovered output invalidates the transition.
- BRC-161 `mint` outputs require valid authority. Its `transfer` and `burn`
  totals require value-input coverage even when authority is present. Legacy
  `burn` outputs have no spendable token value and MUST NOT enter custody or
  incoming-value accounting; explicit and implicit burns both require consent.
- With valid authority, BRC-162 minting and authority split, combine, transfer
  and termination follow its rules. Authority contributes zero to value totals.
- Apply each encoding's admission rules to its own output groups. In
  particular, an invalid BRC-161 transfer/burn group does not by itself
  invalidate a separately authorized legacy mint group. The wallet MUST
  reject an action whose intended outputs are not all admitted; it MUST NOT
  treat destruction of an input by an over-output transaction as a successful
  transfer.
- Metadata and decimals never create supply, confer authority, or waive checks.

For each existing asset, the following exact arithmetic determines both
authorization facts and the required plan operations. Let `V` be the sum of
admitted value inputs (never authority or legacy burn inputs), `T` the sum of
BRC-161 `transfer` outputs, `E` the sum of BRC-161 explicit `burn` outputs,
`M` the sum of BRC-161 `mint` outputs, and `B` the sum of BRC-162 value
outputs. All sums use an integer domain wide enough for multiple uint64
amounts. A legacy `mint` requires an admitted authority input. A legacy
`transfer` or `burn` requires `T + E <= V` even with authority. Put
`R = V - T - E`; a negative `R` fails. Without authority, `M` and new
authority outputs are invalid and `B <= R` is required. With authority, `B`
may exceed `R` under BRC-162. Define:

```text
fundedBinary = min(B, R)
minted       = M + (B - fundedBinary)       // M requires authority
burned       = E + (R - fundedBinary)
transferred  = T + fundedBinary
```

These are canonical **authorization and supply-accounting** quantities, not
an assertion that individual indistinguishable binary output units have an
on-chain provenance. In particular, the convention allocates remaining value
inputs to binary outputs before counting binary minting, avoiding arbitrary
simultaneous mint and burn claims. It preserves `minted - burned =
M + T + B - V`. Legacy `mint` is always newly created supply; legacy
`burn` is always destroyed supply. A positive `burned` amount requires
explicit burn consent and a `burn` operation, even when authority is present
and even if a separate mint makes the net supply change zero. A positive
`minted` amount requires mint consent and a `mint` operation. A positive
`transferred` amount requires `transfer`. Zero quantities require none of
those respective operations. Deploys use only `deploy-fixed` or
`deploy-authority` for their new asset and do not enter this existing-asset
calculation. This calculation does not waive the per-encoding admission rules.

For an existing asset with `A_in > 0` admitted authority inputs, count all
authority outputs for that same asset as `A_out`. Exactly one authority
operation is required: `authority-end` if `A_out = 0`,
`authority-combine` if `0 < A_out < A_in`,
`authority-transfer` if `A_out = A_in`, or
`authority-split` if `A_out > A_in`. These names classify the
transaction-wide capability count; they do not imply that all authorities
remain under one owner or that a same-count replacement is harmless. Consent
MUST separately cover every resulting authority's script, controller where
verifiable, and capability, including delegation and loss of control. Without
an authority input, any new authority output is invalid. Operations for a
given asset MUST be exactly the set implied by these predicates, with no
missing or extra entries. This applies equally to foreign inputs and outputs.

The wallet MUST perform independent satoshi conservation and fee validation.
There is no module-wide one-satoshi rule: service admission policy and actual
script consensus rules determine accepted satoshi values. Nonzero bare-satoshi
payments, token-carrier satoshis and fees remain fully accounted for.

Every completed transaction MUST pass consensus-valid script execution for all
inputs, including arbitrary foreign scripts. An incomplete subject may omit
unlocking scripts only in an explicitly pending action; its available context,
source evidence, overlay state and token transition must still be checked.
Invalid provided scripts MUST fail. The wallet MUST NOT represent a pending
transaction as fully validated, admitted, mined or immediately spendable.

## Structured authorization facts

Authorization MUST be based on trusted, validated facts for the entire action:

- authenticated originator, network and selected wallet/root context;
- every asset's issuance ID, encoding, value and authority inputs and outputs,
  scripts, outpoints, overlay topic and current check results;
- per-asset wallet input total, wallet-owned output total, incoming amount,
  outgoing amount, net debit or credit, burn, mint and authority changes;
- satoshi inputs, wallet receipts, outgoing payments, carrier values and fees;
- recipients as verified control identities where known, otherwise script-based
  destinations with the limits of that identification stated;
- wallet-selected inputs, generated change, caller-fixed positions, sequences,
  locktime, transaction version, signature modes and exact commitment coverage;
- completeness, pending status and terms that remain conditional or mutable.

Netting MUST be computed separately for each asset from authenticated inputs and
wallet-controlled outputs. Foreign funding can offset a debit only to the extent
it validly funds wallet-owned outputs of that **same** asset. Recognizing an
output's key requires the stored or supplied remittance tuple and comparison
against its actual P2PKH hash; an application annotation cannot establish wallet
ownership. Satoshis are accounted separately under normal spending permissions,
including [BRC-116](./0116.md) DSAP where implemented. Token-carrier satoshis MUST
appear in total satoshi accounting without also being counted as a bare-satoshi
payment. Internal transfers and change are not external spending.

When value of one asset leaves and another enters, the wallet MUST present the
action as a swap with all legs. Bare satoshis are an asset for this presentation;
a swap may have any number of token and satoshi legs, including three or more
assets. Different assets MUST NOT cancel each other's debits or disappear from
the presentation. Burns and authority delegation, duplication or destruction
require clear consent even when the fungible net debit is zero. Display metadata
is untrusted descriptive information; the issuance ID remains available.

The user MUST consent to the actual authorized consequences. Rendering and
permission organization are wallet-specific: wallets MAY use per-action approval,
origin-scoped limits, monthly allowances, thresholds, manifest-informed rules or
other user-authorized policies. This specification mandates no particular quota,
permission-token encoding, UI framework, or frequency of prompts. A generic
cryptographic protocol grant does not substitute for asset spending consent.
Concurrent operations and retries MUST NOT exceed a wallet's granted permission
or consume an allowance twice for the same authorized action.

## Metadata types and validation

Every envelope, plan, remittance, operation and constraint is a JSON object
with exactly the named members. Duplicate JSON members and unknown members MUST
fail at every nesting level. The optional `tokenProofs` member is permitted only
in signature envelopes and action plans; omission means an empty array. Receipt
custom instructions MUST reject that member, even when its value is empty. Each item
is exactly `{assetId, subjects, beef}`: a canonical asset ID, a nonempty array
of unique canonical subject outpoints, and a nonempty numeric byte array encoding
one BRC-176 packet. The packet MUST be validated for exactly those subjects and
that asset; contradictory or duplicate proof scopes fail. Packet presence does
not assert current admission or spendability. These bytes count toward the
containing request's resource bound. Wallet-fetched proofs may be used only after
the same validation. The `tx` field separately uses BRC-95 subject framing and
ancestor inclusion; incomplete subject script execution is deferred until
completion, and MUST NOT be described as fully validated Atomic BEEF.

Arrays MUST have the types specified here; `null`
is allowed only where explicitly stated. Strings MUST be strict UTF-8. Numeric
indices and versions MUST be integers, not fractions, booleans or strings.
`version` is exactly `1`. An index is in `0..2^32-1` and MUST select an actual
input/output or a satisfiable planned position. `sighashType` is an integer in
`0..255`, further subject to consensus rules. `tx` is a nonempty numeric array
of integer bytes in `0..255`, not base64 or a numeric-key object.

All metadata `outpoint` fields use canonical lowercase BRC-36 dot form with a
uint32 decimal index without leading zeroes. `assetId` and `ISSUE` use the exact
identity rules above; ISSUE is prohibited for existing inputs. Topics are exact
strings derived from the asset ID. `senderIdentityKey` is a valid compressed
secp256k1 public key. Prefix/suffix strings and resulting derivation must satisfy
the namespace rules. `ownedOutputs` contains at most one remittance per
output index. Source-output classification and topic derivation are entirely
the wallet's responsibility. `inputTopics` and its former hint objects are
not members of any version-1 envelope, plan or receipt instruction and MUST
fail as unknown metadata. Every remittance MUST match authenticated
transaction bytes.

Plan `assets` is an array of unique strings and MUST exactly enumerate token
assets found in the planned sources and outputs, including ISSUE only when
creating a deploy. `operations` is an array of unique `{assetId, operation}` pairs;
`operation` is one of the table's enumerated strings. Each constraint array
contains objects of exactly the stated shape. Duplicate outpoints, asset change
constraints or target positions within an array MUST fail. Cross-array conflicts
and positions incompatible with requested output order MUST fail. Override
outpoints MUST identify managed inputs actually consumed. A selected input
without an override uses the default signature mode. Any malformed metadata
MUST fail without signing, mutation of balances or receipt acceptance.

## Signature requests and composability

An application requests one module-key signature using existing fields:

```text
createSignature({ protocolID, keyID: "color", counterparty,
  data: UTF8(JSON({version:1, tx:[...Atomic BEEF bytes...], inputIndex,
    sighashType, ownedOutputs:[...remittances...]})),
  seekPermission }, originator)
```

The five JSON members shown are required; `ownedOutputs` may be empty.
An optional `tokenProofs` member carries lineage packets as defined below. A
remittance is `{outputIndex, assetId, derivationPrefix, derivationSuffix,
senderIdentityKey}`; `assetId` may be `ISSUE` only for a deploy output. Root
selection for newly managed outputs is the action's selected root; mixed-root
custody MUST NOT be inferred. Additional JSON members, duplicate members,
invalid UTF-8, invalid byte arrays and invalid indices MUST fail.
`hashToDirectlySign` MUST be absent. The envelope is limited to 16 MiB before
allocation; a documented lower host limit MUST fail explicitly rather than
truncate. All remittances are checked, not trusted.

The wallet MUST verify that the protocol's asset selector identifies the selected
source asset; ISSUE is valid only for the preserved original deploy tuple.
It MUST derive the selected key, match it to the selected managed P2PKH
source (or a recognized module-key signing role in a foreign script), construct
the exact consensus preimage from authenticated source data and authorize its
coverage. A foreign source requiring another module's or a BRC-29 key is not an
alias into this signer. Executed `OP_CODESEPARATOR`, when applicable, MUST be
handled using its actual executed script path and consensus script code; a
caller-provided code separator offset or signature slot is not evidence of that
path. If partial foreign execution needs missing material to determine the
preimage, the request fails until that material is supplied. This does not limit
foreign scripts that the module need not sign.

The result is BRC-100 `{signature: Byte[]}`: DER low-S ECDSA over the
wallet-computed transaction digest, without the script sighash byte. Verification
uses `hashToDirectlyVerify` and the same tuple, not the JSON envelope as `data`.
The wallet MUST reserve managed inputs and record consent before releasing a
signature. A signature request may spend module-managed balances; it MUST NOT
silently sign default-basket funding inputs.

### All signature modes

The module MUST support all consensus-valid sighash **families**, including
ALL, NONE, SINGLE and ANYONECANPAY combinations and the post-Chronicle digest
variants. It MUST NOT impose the earlier four-mode restriction. It MUST,
however, refuse to create or release a SINGLE signature unless
`inputIndex < transaction.outputs.length`, even where consensus would accept
the missing-output case. This safety condition applies to `createSignature`,
`createAction` and `signAction`, including signatures prepared for a pending
action. In the original transaction digest algorithm, a missing matching
output makes SINGLE sign the constant digest `1`, so the signature is
reusable without an outpoint or output commitment. Refusing that degenerate
context loses no distinct useful transaction commitment; NONE remains
available where zero output coverage is intended. Other invalid consensus
modes or contexts fail through consensus validation, not a token-specific
mode whitelist. Standard wallet-generated managed-input signatures SHOULD use
ALL|FORKID without ANYONECANPAY unless the authorized agreement needs another
mode.

The wallet MUST select the actual consensus digest algorithm before calculating
coverage. FORKID without CHRONICLE uses the BIP143-style preimage:
`hashPrevouts` commits all input outpoints only when ANYONECANPAY is absent,
and ANYONECANPAY zeros that component. CHRONICLE selects the original
transaction digest algorithm even when the FORKID bit is also set; it does
not have a `hashPrevouts` field. The wallet MUST apply that algorithm's script
code, sequence, input and output serialization rules, including executed
`OP_CODESEPARATOR`, before signing or describing commitments. ALL commits
all outputs, a valid SINGLE commits its corresponding output, and NONE commits
no outputs. These components MUST NOT be described as interchangeable. A
named, unspent counterparty input committed by a signature prevents a
completed transaction from consuming it after a conflicting spend; that fact
alone does not commit the requested outputs or guarantee token delivery.

A wallet MAY release a partially signed offer before receiving the counterparty's
unlocking scripts. For a transaction-level guaranteed swap, the named incoming
inputs and promised wallet-owned outputs MUST be committed by the released
signature, validated covenant conditions, or other independently verified
commitments that remain binding in every valid completion. The wallet MUST
identify uncommitted terms and all possible authorized consequences, including
burn, missing consideration and authority delegation. Disclosure or broad
consent alone MUST NOT turn an unsafe conditional exchange into a guaranteed
swap. A weaker signature mode MAY be used when the user's authorized disposition
is unconditional at signature release, so every possible completion is within
that disposition and a later untrusted actor cannot worsen the user's agreed
position, or when the wallet has verified an applicable, system-specific
safeguard or remedy for the conditional exchange.
The safeguard or remedy may be in script, at law, or elsewhere in the asset
ecosystem; its design is outside this BRC. If neither condition holds, the
wallet MUST decline the signature. It MUST NOT present conditional terms as
guaranteed receipts or authorize only the displayed favorable completion.

Revalidation before wallet completion is mandatory but cannot restrict an
external party using an already released signature. A pending reference, UI
cancellation or expired allowance does not retract that capability. Composable
agreements remain possible with arbitrary scripts and independently supplied
signatures; the wallet enforces accurate authorization rather than a fixed swap
transaction template.

Transaction-level commitments do not themselves establish overlay admission.
Before signature release or broadcast, the wallet MUST apply its configured
asset and counterparty acceptance policy to the intended transaction, including
available admission preflight, participant conventions and any system-specific
settlement safeguard. A wallet MUST NOT claim that an output is a guaranteed
admitted token receipt before the required topic accepts it. Because later
provider failure, policy differences or another party's inaction can defeat a
prospective credit, designers of a conditional exchange MUST bind the required
consideration or provide a clearly specified alternate remedy under that
system's assumptions and risk profile. Otherwise a participant who releases
an inadequately constrained signature may lose **that participant's** assets.
One participant's signature grants no power over another participant's keys:
each independently contributed input requires its controller's own valid
authorization, and recovery or overlay disagreement MUST NOT treat unrelated
users' assets as spendable or invalid. This BRC does not prescribe scripts,
certificates, on- or off-chain identity linkability, legal terms, or an
asset-specific remedy.

## Action planning, selection and change

Ordinary BRC-100 calls keep their existing argument and result shapes. The
module action plan is strict UTF-8 JSON, hex encoded and split into consecutive
chunks of at most 200 hex characters. It is carried in existing `labels`:

```text
p color plan <partIndex> <partCount> <hexChunk>
```

Hex chunks MUST contain an even, nonzero number of lowercase hex digits.
Indices are zero-based; index and count are decimal without leading zeroes and
bounded to uint32. Concatenate chunks by index, decode hex once, then decode
strict UTF-8 JSON with duplicate-member rejection. Nonfinal chunks MUST contain
exactly 200 hex characters; the final chunk contains at most 200. Reject missing
parts, inconsistent counts, invalid encodings or bounds before large allocation.
There MUST be exactly one complete plan, with each part present once and a
consistent positive count. Parts may occur among ordinary action labels; their
index determines reconstruction. A plan is bounded to 16 MiB decoded JSON and
existing per-label limits. A host with lower action limits MUST report an error.
Plan labels are module metadata and MUST NOT be interpreted as consent or as
[BRC-177](./0177.md) expiry labels. `listActions` queries using these exact labels
retain ordinary matching semantics with module-aware read permissions.

The plan has these required members and the optional `tokenProofs` member
defined above, with no other members:

| Member | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `version` | Integer `1`. |
| `assets` | Unique canonical asset IDs involved, plus `ISSUE` for a deploy. |
| `ownedOutputs` | Remittances for requested outputs to be managed; same format as the signature envelope. |
| `operations` | Per-asset intent: `transfer`, `burn`, `deploy-fixed`, `deploy-authority`, `mint`, `authority-split`, `authority-combine`, `authority-transfer`, or `authority-end`; declare every applicable operation and validate against actual scripts. |
| `inputPositions` | Array of `{outpoint, index}` constraints on explicit inputs; empty when unconstrained. |
| `changePositions` | Array of `{assetId, index}` constraints for generated token change, or `{assetId:null,index}` for ordinary satoshi change; empty when unconstrained. |
| `sighashTypes` | Array of `{outpoint, sighashType}` overrides for managed inputs; empty uses the default above. |

An operation is `{assetId, operation}`. Authority operations and burns must be
explicit; inferred transfer intent never authorizes them. A plan's `ISSUE`
identifies only its deploy, not any existing asset. The transaction's prefix and
validated sources determine quantities and operations; the plan cannot override
them. A deploy plan MUST have exactly one deploy. After finalization, its record
is indexed under the resulting ID without changing the signed plan or derivation.

`createAction` MUST recognize plan labels, token-encoded requested outputs,
relevant module baskets, and explicit inputs already managed by the module. Missing required plans or source evidence fail.
The module MUST authenticate foreign inputs, select sufficient unreserved value
inputs separately per asset, and make fresh per-asset change outputs under the
custody profile. Valid foreign amounts are netted before wallet selection.
Authority operations select actual authority inputs, not a numeric balance.
Applications MAY supply required inputs or input/output positions. Setting
`options.randomizeOutputs:false` preserves requested output order; explicit
position constraints and generated change MUST be satisfied without inserting,
shuffling or replacing covenant-sensitive outputs. An impossible layout,
insufficient asset or satoshi balance, or absent overlay membership MUST fail
before signing. Newly generated keys and authoritative ownership facts belong to
the wallet; application selection does not make an input foreign.

Only declared burns may suppress value change; mint and issuance outputs follow
their declared supply rules. Satoshi funding, outputs and change use the ordinary
wallet action machinery alongside the token plan. The wallet MUST authorize the
final funded layout, not an earlier estimate. `createAction` MAY automatically
sign managed P2PKH inputs when that final action is valid and authorized, including
colored-coin payments and multi-asset swaps. Missing foreign unlocking scripts
or `signAndProcess:false` returns the existing `signableTransaction` reference.
Applications may exchange the resulting partial transaction and signatures.

`signAction` MUST preserve the pending plan, reservations, constraints and
previously committed fields; validate supplied unlocking scripts; refresh overlay
spendability and permission validity; and validate the final transaction before
returning it as complete or processing it. A changed context needs revalidation
and consent covering the change, with new signatures where required. A module
signature and an action reference for the same inputs MUST share one authoritative
reservation and authorization record rather than conflict or charge twice.

Reservations are atomic across all assets and satoshi funding. Failed checks
before signature release roll back provisional reservations. After signature
release, aborting an action may cancel processing but MUST retain the relevant
exposure record; inputs MUST NOT be offered to a conflicting action until the
wallet establishes that the capability can no longer consume them, or explicitly
authorizes a linked revision or conflicting spend while retaining all remaining
exposure. Authorization alone does not invalidate the earlier offer. Unspentness
checks, observed external completion and conflicts update this record. Wallets
MUST distinguish pending exposure from settled spending. Quota implementation is
wallet-specific, but released capabilities cannot be hidden by resetting a
pending action. `noSend`, `sendWith` and ordinary two-phase flows remain supported;
returning a noSend transaction does not establish overlay admission.

### Non-final transactions, revisions and auctions

Wallets MUST support time- or height-constrained Colored Token actions through
the existing BRC-100 `lockTime`, input `sequenceNumber`, `signableTransaction`,
`signAction` and `noSend` fields. This applies to multi-asset auctions, token-for-
satoshi bids and arbitrary foreign contract scripts as well as ordinary sends.
The application supplies auction terms; the wallet validates their enforceable
consequences and owns the processor coordination required below. This defines
no new action method or Bitcoin consensus rule.

Track these facts separately: whether all unlocking scripts are valid and
present; whether the transaction is consensus-final in the evaluated chain
context; whether processing/broadcast is requested and currently eligible; and
whether every required overlay has admitted it. Fully signed bytes have a
transaction ID even while non-final. An incomplete transaction's current ID is
provisional. Neither type of pending action supplies spendable received value.

Apply Bitcoin's [transaction finality rules](https://protocol.bsvblockchain.org/specification/consensus-rules#finality-nlocktime--nsequence)
to the complete input set. `lockTime` is an absolute height below 500000000 and
an absolute timestamp otherwise. A zero lock time, a lock time strictly below
the applicable chain comparison value, or all input sequences equal to
4294967295 makes the transaction final. A lower sequence alone does not create
a delay; all-final sequences bypass even a future lock time. Implementations
MUST use the applicable chain rules, not the user's wall clock or a Bitcoin
relative-locktime assumption. An auction deadline is not enforced merely by
putting a timestamp in metadata or using a nonmaximum sequence.

Before releasing a signature, establish and present the effective time/height
constraint, all asset and satoshi consequences, output positions, signature
commitments, and any still-executable earlier revisions. A wallet MAY authorize
an explicitly pending non-final action once all available scripts, sources,
current overlay state and token transitions pass validation. It MUST NOT claim
immediate settlement or ordinary completed processing while non-final. For a
fully signed non-final action, `noSend:true` may return its transaction bytes;
the wallet MUST retain its pending status. A processing request that cannot yet
be fulfilled MUST return an explicit error and preserve released exposure.
With `noSend:false` and `acceptDelayedBroadcast:true`, a complete non-final
action MAY instead succeed as processor-accepted pending processing, but only
after the coordination acceptance checks below. With delayed processing
disallowed, it MUST return an error while non-final. Processor acceptance is
not settlement or spendable overlay admission.

Each revision is an exact transaction context. `signAction.spends[i].sequenceNumber`
may update that input only subject to BRC-100's saved-action contract; it cannot
silently alter other inputs, outputs or lock time. A changed bid price, recipient,
lock time or layout needs a new `createAction` context (or the existing complete
signature-request envelope), linked by the wallet to the shared input exposure.
Every revision MUST recheck all assets, sources, conservation, permissions,
positions and current overlay state, and recompute every affected preimage.
Retain a signature only if it verifies for the revised context; regenerate or
obtain every invalidated signature before returning a complete transaction.
This includes foreign signatures: changing a sequence does not give the wallet
authority or ability to replace another party's signature. Preserve covenant
output ordering and generated-change constraints, or fail.

#### Wallet-managed processor coordination

A conforming wallet MUST establish and maintain coordination with transaction
processors that ensures **every valid higher-sequence replacement is accepted**
while the registered transaction family remains replaceable. This is the
wallet's responsibility, including processor selection, authentication,
submission, propagation, acknowledgement, recovery and ongoing coverage. An
application need not implement processor-specific replacement logic. A wallet
MUST NOT shift this obligation to the application, substitute a risk disclosure
for it, or silently downgrade a coordinated revision to best-effort broadcast.

Before first releasing a non-final transaction signature, including through
`createSignature`, partial actions and `noSend`, the wallet MUST establish an
authenticated processor arrangement covering the family and its intended
lifetime. The arrangement MUST identify the participating processors, their
replacement and retention obligations, the evidence of acceptance, and their
capacity, fee and availability conditions. The wallet MUST arrange sufficient
coverage for the promised replacement and settlement behavior; merely choosing
an arbitrary reachable node is insufficient. It MUST check these conditions
before authorizing a revision, so a valid authorized replacement cannot later be
treated as an optional policy exception. Without sufficient coordination, the
wallet MUST fail before releasing a new signature. This BRC standardizes the
obligation and validated facts, not a vendor, fixed processor count or transport.

A **transaction family** is identified by the network and the exact ordered
input outpoint list, held in the wallet's shared reservation record. Relative
to the latest accepted revision, a higher-sequence replacement MUST preserve
that list, decrease no input's uint32 sequence and increase at least one.
Identical bytes are an idempotent resubmission, not a new revision. Different
bytes with the same sequence vector, decreasing or incomparable vectors MUST
NOT supersede an accepted revision. The wallet MUST serialize competing updates
against the latest accepted state rather than compare each with a stale base.
Changing the input set or order is a new family, and requires a separately
validated coordination handoff covering any shared-input exposure; it is not
made a valid replacement by increasing an unrelated sequence. A handoff MUST
bind both families, their latest accepted transaction IDs and shared outpoints;
all required processors must acknowledge the new family as successor and prevent
the earlier family from resuming preferred processing before success is reported.
If these obligations cannot be established, the handoff fails without silently
freeing the old family's inputs.

A **valid replacement** also has the required valid unlocking scripts, current
source availability and token evidence, exact asset and satoshi accounting,
authorized terms, and preserved positional/covenant constraints. Partial bytes
may be prepared under the arrangement but cannot be reported as accepted complete
replacements. Higher sequence is not permission to change the bid price or any
other term. The wallet MUST regenerate or obtain affected signatures as above.
Replacement eligibility closes when an input is consumed by an established
conflicting spend, the latest accepted revision becomes consensus-final in the
evaluated chain context, or the processors atomically accept an expressly
authorized finalizing revision. An arbitrary local timeout or a processor
refusal is not finalization. The arrangement MUST cover the non-final lifetime;
a processor may not unilaterally shorten it. A valid higher-sequence finalizing
revision is accepted before closing the family to further updates. A sequence
increase cannot undo completed settlement. The wallet MUST manage propagation
and finalization timing so it does not promise acceptance of an update whose
acceptance window it cannot satisfy.

When processing is requested, the wallet MUST obtain authenticated evidence that
the exact transaction is accepted as the latest revision under the arrangement,
superseding earlier revisions for relay and eventual processing. Evidence MUST
bind the network, ordered input outpoints, previous and replacement transaction
IDs (no previous ID for initial acceptance), sequence vector, processor identities
and the arrangement's validity scope.
Required processors MUST agree on the accepted revision, retain or recover that
state across restarts, and prevent stale or equal-sequence conflicting revisions
from becoming preferred again. An HTTP success, one ordinary mempool observation,
an overlay answer, or an application's assertion is not sufficient evidence of
those obligations. The wallet MUST monitor their continued fulfillment until
finalization, and coordinate replacement processors and overlays as necessary.

With `noSend:true` the wallet MUST honor the prohibition on transaction
submission: it may establish the arrangement without submitting the signed
transaction, but returns **prepared**, not processor-accepted, bytes. The same
distinction applies to `signableTransaction`. When the caller later requests
processing through `signAction` or a `sendWith` flow, the wallet performs the
acceptance step. It MUST NOT claim replacement from the mere return of a
signature, transaction ID or pending reference.

Processor refusal, timeout, conflicting acknowledgements or lost coverage MUST
produce an explicit failure of the replacement operation, not a successful
latest-revision result. The wallet MUST preserve all transmitted or released
exposure, reconcile uncertain outcomes and retry idempotently where possible.
A valid update's refusal is a failure to fulfill the coordination obligation,
not permission to settle an older revision silently. The wallet MUST surface
that failure and MUST NOT finalize a stale revision or credit speculative
outputs while the accepted state is unresolved. Changing providers MUST preserve
the latest accepted state and obligations rather than reset the sequence history.

This guarantee is supplied by the wallet's processor arrangement. It does not
make an earlier transaction's signatures cryptographically invalid, change
Bitcoin's finality rules, or establish that a higher sequence is a higher price.
The wallet MUST retain earlier released capabilities as historical exposure,
including after coordinated supersession, until a verified conflict or an
independently verified consensus/script condition prevents their use. The user
and application can rely on the wallet to enforce replacement within its
arrangement; they are not responsible for recreating that coordination. A
highest-bid-wins promise additionally requires validation of the auction's bid
ordering and authorization rules.

`lockTime` remains an earliest-inclusion constraint, not signature expiry or a
last-valid auction time. Neither abort nor noSend expiry cancels the processors'
obligations or erases released capabilities. Cancellation MUST reconcile the
coordinated state, preserve exposure and report any cancellation that cannot be
established; it MUST NOT pretend that local deletion revoked the transaction.

On finalization, recheck chain context, all scripts, permissions, all asset
states and the processors' agreed latest revision, then follow normal processing
and overlay admission. Do not silently set
sequences to 4294967295, reduce lock time, drop fixed outputs or reuse stale
authorization to make a transaction final. An unspentness check can fail because
an earlier revision or another spender consumed an input; report that conflict
and reconcile reservations without crediting competing prospective outputs.
These rules apply equally to internalization: a fully signed but non-final
receipt MUST fail, and overlay acknowledgement cannot waive consensus finality.

## Internalization

`internalizeAction` uses the existing `basket insertion` protocol with the
module's per-asset basket and `insertionRemittance.customInstructions` containing
strict JSON `{version:1, assetId, derivationPrefix, derivationSuffix,
senderIdentityKey}` with no additional members. A deploy receipt uses
`assetId: "ISSUE"` for key
derivation and derives its actual asset ID and basket from the supplied completed
transaction. The root is the wallet's selected root. Unknown or duplicate members
and missing required fields MUST fail. An incorrect basket MUST fail rather than
be silently reclassified; an issuance initiated by this wallet uses its automatic
final-ID assignment. `paymentRemittance` for ordinary satoshis remains BRC-29.

The complete `customInstructions` string MUST fit BRC-100's 1000 UTF-8 byte
limit. `tokenProofs`, proof URLs and proof-reference fields are not receipt
members; they MUST be rejected rather than ignored. Receipts obtain historical
admission through the defined lookup, or independently verified BRC-176 lineage
already held by the wallet or retrieved under its configured provider and
evidence policy. Fetched or cached packets MUST meet the same exact asset,
subject, completeness and chain-evidence checks as caller-supplied packets in
action plans or signature envelopes. Cache presence alone is not validation.
If sufficient evidence is unavailable, internalization fails. Current membership
of the received output is still required in every case.

This defines no new caller proof-upload transport. The ordinary `tx` field
retains its BRC-95 meaning: a BRC-176 packet MUST NOT be smuggled through it or
stripped as ordinary Atomic BEEF. Removing receipt-embedded proofs changes their
delivery, not the wallet's validation obligations.

Before accepting a receipt the wallet MUST validate complete transaction scripts,
source evidence, foreign-input authentication, historical topical admission
of consumed inputs, per-asset amount and authority rules, and its control of each
requested custody output. It MUST NOT demand current unspentness of inputs
already consumed by that receipt.
The same encoding, lineage, amount and ownership checks apply to foreign inputs
used by `createAction` and prospective wallet-owned outputs used in signing
accounting. A candidate output is not yet admitted: its prospective credit is
conditional on transaction completion and overlay acceptance, and MUST NOT be
added to spendable local balance before receipt admission. The received outpoint itself MUST
already appear as admitted and unspent in its asset's spendability lookup.
Internalization MUST NOT silently submit an absent transaction to make this test
pass. Missing overlay admission MUST reject internalization. The sender or
higher-level flow is responsible for submission before requesting receipt.

For wallet-created completed, consensus-final actions, normal processing MUST submit the
transaction to every involved token topic and record each topic's actual
admission result. A `noSend` or partial action defers submission. Coordinated
processing of a complete non-final action may submit it to transaction processors
as specified above, but defers ordinary final processing and spendable overlay
credit. Deployments first follow the discovery-topic bootstrap above. Admission into
one asset's overlay does not imply admission into another's; local receipt and
spendable balances MUST only be credited after all required admission checks.
If Bitcoin completion occurs but an overlay operation fails, record the actual
pending/error state without claiming reversal of the on-chain spend. Retries MUST
be idempotent. Partial or consensus-non-final transactions cannot be internalized as completed receipts.

## Discovery, compatibility and security boundaries

This specification defines the optional [BRC-205](./0205.md) attribute
`brc207-color`. Its sole decoded value is `1`, meaning the wallet implements this
version's `color` protocol, basket and action-plan contract. Example:

```text
example-1.2.3; brc207-color=1;
```

Encoding, single occurrence and outer bounds follow BRC-205. Unknown values MUST
be ignored as unsupported metadata. A wallet MAY omit the attribute for privacy,
while locked, or to meet bounds; omission is not proof of non-support. Presence
is a snapshot, not authorization, proof of safe code or core-method negotiation.
It MUST contain no user-specific balances, grants, asset inventory or history.
Consumers SHOULD refresh on the selected connection before starting a workflow,
and MUST discard cached values on provider/account changes or disconnection.
Every actual call still checks current support and permissions. BRC-205's
extended-string compatibility configuration is required before emission; legacy
paths continue to return base-only versions. BRC-185 discovery is independent.
BRC-185 and BRC-205 are unmerged companion specifications at publication of this
revision; canonical links identify their intended locations after merge.
Implementations MUST independently adopt BRC-205's profile before using this
attribute; this document does not assert deployment or adoption of that profile.

The module supports the BRC-161/162 token model; it does not assume compatibility
with earlier `p mandala` drafts, BTMS, generic application keys or other basket profiles.
Migration and recovery are outside this specification. An application MUST NOT
strip the reserved prefix or fall back to generic signing when this module is
unavailable. No software implementation, universal deployment, or live-wallet
conformance is asserted here.

Overlay trust is explicit and independent of SPV and Bitcoin consensus. A
current spendability answer cannot promise future availability; consensus input
commitments and script conditions establish completion constraints. Metadata,
permissions and partial transaction references cannot change those
constraints. Trusted module code retains private keys and validates the facts
supplied to wallet policy and presentation. Arbitrary foreign scripts retain
composability without receiving access to internal wallet signing capabilities.

## Conformance cases

| Case | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Same identities and remittance tuple | Sender-derived recipient public key matches recipient-derived private key; exact encoding and owner selection agree. |
| Deploy finalized | Asset ID and value/authority basket assigned from final outpoint; ISSUE-derived key preserved. |
| BRC-161 and BRC-162 inputs for one ID | One asset account, encoding-specific validation, BRC-162 generated outputs. |
| Unresolved source, service substitution, missing overlay output | Fail without signing or crediting. |
| Foreign input provenance | Reconstruct and verify source; matching current topic admission required before amount netting. |
| Split, merge, burn, mint and all authority operations | Exact per-asset rules and consent; no authority-as-value arithmetic. |
| Three-asset swap or token-for-satoshis swap | Every leg and satoshi fee represented independently. |
| Wallet selection and change with fixed positions | Sufficient eligible inputs and fresh change; preserve constraints or fail. |
| Partial offer before foreign unlocks | Pending status, verified sources, accurate commitments and authorization of possible outcomes. |
| ALL, NONE, SINGLE and ANYONECANPAY | Consensus preimage correctness; no fictitious hashPrevouts or output coverage. |
| SINGLE with no corresponding output | Reject for both BIP143 and Chronicle digest variants; never release the original-algorithm constant-digest signature. |
| Mixed legacy/binary value with authority | Apply the exact input-allocation arithmetic; declare and consent to every positive mint, burn and transfer, including offsetting mint and burn. |
| Completed foreign-script spend | Full consensus script validation before use or processing. |
| Internalization absent from overlay | Reject; do not silently submit. |
| Retry, concurrent requests, abort and noSend | Shared reservations and accurate exposure; no duplicate credits or quota charges. |
| Inventory and exact balances | Discover value, authority and pending/reserved records without treating them all as available; preserve exact aggregates or error. |
| Coordinated sequence replacement | Wallet arranges processor coverage before signature release; a valid higher revision must be accepted when processing is requested, or an explicit failure preserves exposure. |
| Receipt metadata bounds | No embedded tokenProofs; enforce 1000 UTF-8 bytes and obtain required evidence independently. |
| Capability omitted or stale | Handle actual call results; no generic-signing fallback. |

## Appendix A — Conformance Examples

### A.0. Reading and running the examples

These independent examples use the BRC-100 CWI cross-document JSON substrate.
Each request and response below is the complete JSON message at that boundary.
`type`, `isInvocation`, `id`, `call`, `args`, `status` and `result` are transport
fields, not new wallet-method parameters. The authenticated browser message
source and origin supply the caller identity outside this JSON; examples use
`https://shop.example`. A caller-supplied originator cannot impersonate another
application. A wallet adapter for another BRC-100 substrate carries the same
method arguments and results with that substrate's framing.

Error messages and numerical codes shown here are illustrative CWI mappings:
1 is a general failure, 2 an unsupported action, 6 an invalid parameter, and 7
insufficient funds. Conformance requires the stated failure and lack of
unauthorized side effects, not identical English wording or a new module error
registry. UI text, approval frequency, transaction fee choice, signature nonce,
and reference generation remain wallet choices. Exact result bytes shown are
one valid outcome under the stated fixtures. The user-visible facts and
validation outcomes are required. All amounts below are integer token units
unless explicitly described as satoshis or display values.

Every case starts with a fresh copy of the stated fixture state. A reference to
an earlier case imports its described state or bytes, not changes made by
executing all preceding examples in order. Do not submit these synthetic
transactions to a live network or interpret their identifiers as claims about
existing assets. This is an offline reference fixture: its initial source
transactions are prevalidated, coinbase anchors at distinct heights starting at 100000, matured at a fixture tip of 100200 in a controlled chain
tracker and authoritative wallet store, and the configured test SLAP providers
attest the stated topic states. A test harness must supply that trusted anchor
state; the raw transactions alone are not SPV proofs. A production wallet must
obtain and verify real chain evidence. Txid-only BEEF entries below resolve only
through this authoritative fixture store; cases that remove that knowledge fail.
BRC-176 packets additionally retain the complete token-source bodies.

For reproducible public keys the synthetic ordinary, Bob and privileged root
private integers are respectively 11, 22 and 33. They are public test material,
never production credentials. The anyone counterparty is private integer 1.
Each key-controlled source deploy uses its original ISSUE tuple, keyID `color`, the nonce
prefix listed below and suffix `Ag==`. Subsequent token outputs use their actual
asset selector. A newly selected change nonce is fresh within that case. Default
satoshi outputs use BRC-29 protocol [2, "3241645161d8"], keyID "AQ== AA==",
counterparty anyone and the respective owner root, independent of token keys.

Fixture identities:

```json
{
  "alice": "03774ae7f858a9411e5ef4246b70c65aac5649980be5c17891bbec17895da008cb",
  "bob": "03421f5fc9a21065445c96fdb91c0c1e2f2431741c72713b4b99ddcb316f31e9fc",
  "privileged": "021697ffa6fd9de627c077e3d2fe541084ce13300b0bec1146f95ae57f0d0bd6a5",

}
```
