Complete explicit-input requests include already supplied fixture unlocking scripts,
not unlockingScriptLength. These are signatures from the input owners’ normal
transaction authorization paths; the receiving wallet still checks its own
permissions and the entire final action. They do not demonstrate application
access to protected private keys. The final ordinary change output is generated by the wallet at the specified
changePositions index; applications never select the reserved default basket.
Automatic selection/signing and deferred input-length flows are shown separately.

For successful createAction traces the fixture wallet uses version 1, locktime
0, final input sequences, the shown output order and a 10-satoshi fee, unless
stated otherwise. It uses deterministic low-S signatures for the reproduced
transaction IDs. Normal processing succeeds on Bitcoin and every involved
overlay unless the case states a different result. A successful method result
never by itself asserts mining finality. The fixture scripts and signature
results are concrete encodings; wallet-specific fee/layout choices are allowed
when the application has not fixed them, but must be reflected in consent.

### A.01. Capability discovery

The connection has explicitly adopted a BRC-205-compatible version profile. The application originates this request.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDAx",
  "call": "getVersion",
  "args": {}
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDAx",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "version": "example-1.2.3; brc207-color=1;"
  }
}
```

The application recognizes module version 1 across protocol, basket and label namespaces. This is capability information, not permission to spend or evidence of a user balance.

### A.02. Omitted capability

This wallet supports Colored Tokens but chooses not to advertise it. The connection uses the ordinary core version profile.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDAy",
  "call": "getVersion",
  "args": {}
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDAy",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "version": "example-1.2.3"
  }
}
```

Omission does not establish lack of support. An application can attempt the intended module call and handle its result. It never substitutes generic signing. Account, provider or connection changes discard the previous capability cache; discovery is repeated.

### A.03. Unrecognized metadata

The compatible version profile is enabled.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDAz",
  "call": "getVersion",
  "args": {}
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDAz",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "version": "example-1.2.3; brc207-color=2;"
  }
}
```

The application ignores the unsupported attribute value. It does not interpret version 2 as version 1, enumerate holdings from this response, or use cached authorization after a provider change.

### A.04. Original deploy key

Alice asks for the public key of her preserved ISSUE tuple, using prefix AQ== and suffix Ag==.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA0",
  "call": "getPublicKey",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "forSelf": true
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA0",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "publicKey": "03a3c96df02329cbbf056ee25ee0c24ba36c7620cce9b4eba41972327a12a1f22d"
  }
}
```

The protocol encodes the UTF-8 strings AQ== and Ag== as 41513d3d and 41673d3d. It does not decode their base64 contents. This remains the key of the original deploy after its asset ID becomes known.

### A.05. Counterparty ownership

Alice requests her own child public key with Bob as counterparty. Bob independently derives the recipient key from Alice’s public identity with forSelf:false.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA1",
  "call": "getPublicKey",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "03421f5fc9a21065445c96fdb91c0c1e2f2431741c72713b4b99ddcb316f31e9fc",
    "forSelf": true
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA1",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "publicKey": "03a5a62ba6fe30cda7de3c6f705389ba20dcde671a86a43810aaa14dfcc7c12c1a"
  }
}
```

Bob obtains exactly 03a5a62ba6fe30cda7de3c6f705389ba20dcde671a86a43810aaa14dfcc7c12c1a. The recipient can derive the matching private key. Changing the application origin or basket does not alter this derivation; permission remains origin-aware. A sender uses the recipient identity and forSelf:false, rather than accidentally deriving its own child.

### A.06. Privileged root selection

Alice explicitly authorizes access to the separate privileged fixture root.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA2",
  "call": "getPublicKey",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "forSelf": true,
    "privileged": true,
    "privilegedReason": "Access separate token identity"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA2",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "publicKey": "0222da9b9ede01dc1ae6f1a038d9be2199db5ada2fec3690bca14b0b511ad16dc7"
  }
}
```

This key differs from the ordinary-root key. An output addressed to the ordinary identity is not credited to the privileged root merely because its remittance strings match. The selected root remains consistent throughout one action; mixed-root custody is not inferred.

### A.07. Reject alternate identifier spellings

The application uses the human-readable logical identifier instead of its defined wire encoding.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA3",
  "call": "getPublicKey",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color ISSUE AQ== Ag=="
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "forSelf": true
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA3",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Invalid Colored Tokens protocol identifier",
  "code": 6
}
```

No key is derived. Uppercase selectors, underscore wire asset tags, whitespace-bearing or empty decoded remittances, and over-280-character protocol names likewise fail; the wallet neither truncates nor silently re-encodes them.

### A.08. Wallet without the module

This case uses a core BRC-100 wallet that does not implement Colored Tokens.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA4",
  "call": "getPublicKey",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "forSelf": true
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA4",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Colored Tokens module is not supported",
  "code": 2
}
```

The reserved identifier is rejected before generic permissions. A prior advertisement from another account or provider does not change this result; the application must handle the failure without removing p color.

### A.09. Per-asset inventory

Alice has the admitted, unreserved A deploy output in its assigned value basket.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA5",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04v0",
    "include": "locking scripts"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDA5",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": 1,
    "outputs": [
      {
        "outpoint": "e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04.0",
        "satoshis": 1000,
        "lockingScript": "0002e8036d76a9146cbd20c18911529dd4a707cbe7abcd296a097fe488ac",
        "spendable": true
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The script carries 1000 token units and the UTXO carries 1000 satoshis: these are different quantities. Other assets are not included. The ISSUE derivation remains attached to this output in the wallet’s authoritative records.

### A.10. Authority inventory

Alice holds the admitted H deploy authority, independently of any H value balance.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEw",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6cv0 authority",
    "include": "locking scripts"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEw",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": 1,
    "outputs": [
      {
        "outpoint": "ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6c.0",
        "satoshis": 1000,
        "lockingScript": "00006d76a91475a06c850b20c3c7e7e9d607f2cc64ba885daaed88ac",
        "spendable": true
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The wallet presents one minting authority and its control implications, not a fungible balance of zero units available for payment. It does not combine this inventory with the H value basket.

### A.11. Relinquishing tracking preserves key protection

Alice permits the application to stop tracking A in this basket.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEx",
  "call": "relinquishOutput",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04v0",
    "output": "e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04.0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEx",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "relinquished": true
  }
}
```

The organizational operation does not spend the outpoint or grant cryptographic access. A later module signature still requires full transaction validation and asset consent, even if the application supplies a different basket name or deletes its own metadata.

### A.12. Auxiliary cryptography is unavailable

A generic cryptographic permission was previously granted to this origin.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEy",
  "call": "createHmac",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "data": [1, 2, 3]
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEy",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "HMAC is unavailable for Colored Tokens keys",
  "code": 2
}
```

The reserved protocol dispatch wins over that grant. The same prohibition applies to encryption, decryption, HMAC verification and key-linkage disclosure; a public getPublicKey or verifySignature call does not grant any of these capabilities.

### A.13. A digest is not spend authorization

The application asks the protected key to sign a 32-byte digest directly.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEz",
  "call": "createSignature",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "seekPermission": true,
    "hashToDirectlySign": [17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17, 17]
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDEz",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Colored Tokens signing requires a transaction envelope",
  "code": 6
}
```

No signature is returned, regardless of a generic protocol grant. Supplying a bare serialized preimage as data is also insufficient.

### A.14. Automatic selection, change and signing

Alice owns only A for this asset, and no input is reserved. Her existing policy allows this 300-unit payment and the final satoshi consequences. The wallet’s next fresh change tuple is prefix CA==, suffix Ag==.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "txid": "b47d158600304268e860840a766c551699bf002de2d1760e11fc524d83f91fc6"
  }
}
```

The wallet selects e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04.0, derives its topic from the verified source, and checks current admission. It pays 300 A units to Bob, creates 700 A units of change at output 1, and ordinary satoshi change of 988 at output 2. Total satoshi input is 1000; output carriers are 1 and 1, ordinary change is 988, and the fee is 10. Alice’s token debit is 300 and satoshi debit is 11, of which 1 is Bob’s carrier and 10 is fee. Her own change carrier is not counted again as a bare-satoshi payment. After consensus validation and successful processing on the A topic, the wallet can expose the admitted change as spendable. No cryptographic call by the application is needed.

### A.21. A plan cannot select a lookup service URL

The application adds a lookupURL field to an otherwise valid plan, attempting to choose the provider for A. Provider selection belongs to the wallet configuration.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

### A.37. Over-output value transition

The first output now encodes 301 units while the change still encodes 700. The input contains exactly 1000 and no authority. Losing one unit of coverage would invalidate all value outputs of this asset under BRC-162.

The script amount is exactly 18446744073709551615 units; the display may be 18.446744073709551615 G. Neither JavaScript floating-point rounding nor display decimals may change the integer. The maximum uses a nine-byte minimal positive script number. Aggregates of multiple such inputs use wider exact arithmetic even though each output remains within uint64. An invalid or unknown display payload is ignored for metadata purposes, not used to create supply.

### A.47. Create an offer before the other party signs

The wallet returns a pending reference and a partially signed subject: Alice’s input is signed, Bob’s is empty. Sources, current overlays and token amounts have been validated, but the transaction cannot yet complete. The wallet reserves A and records the released capability; it does not credit B or claim overlay admission. The signature binds transaction terms, while admission still depends on both topics. Bob’s future signature can be exchanged separately. This response also illustrates signAndProcess:false without a new core API.

### A.48. Complete the pending swap

Start with the independently established pending offer described in A.47. Both named token sources remain admitted and unspent, and the permission still covers the offer. Bob supplies his exact final script.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json

  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDQ4",

  "args": {

    "spends": {

        "unlockingScript": "483045022100f3e0efe667230ecd9f750e5c1c3348ee89dadd25cff11ad70ed90a0e131d7ab402200119a0d91d3d766a29fbc23f1ac07a20d6fb01f05306ec1870f59f79256d403c4121035426266ad18afddbd6997b724b619249f334d259430e9733b8fa92f8a6153ecb"
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDQ4",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "txid": "a85bb1cd5778cdd0c8606f6c640e96c2ad5779579ee6f6dad0edbf1c00360196"
  }
}
```

The wallet preserves the pending plan and previously committed fields, refreshes every involved overlay, validates both complete input scripts, and processes the transaction on both asset topics. It consumes the existing authorization record once. It records actual admission results before making incoming B spendable.

### A.49. Counterparty input spent before completion

Start with the pending offer in A.47, but Bob’s named B outpoint has now been consumed by a different valid transaction.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
      }
    },

      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDQ5",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Counterparty token input is no longer spendable",
  "code": 1
}
```

The wallet fails completion and does not replace the named input or reuse Alice’s signature on changed terms. Because Alice’s released signature commits that outpoint, the old offer cannot complete after the conflict. The wallet updates exposure once the conflict is authoritatively established.

### A.50. Reject an invalid completion script

Start with the pending offer in A.47. The caller supplies an invalid P2PKH unlocking script for Bob.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDUw",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "bWFuZGFsYS1zd2FwLTAwMDE=",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "51"
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDUw",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Input script validation failed",
  "code": 6
}
```

The action remains pending; no completed transaction is returned or processed. Alice’s released capability and reservation are retained. Missing foreign scripts may be deferred in a partial offer, but invalid supplied scripts cannot be accepted as a completed spend.

### A.51. Previously committed fields cannot change silently

Start with the pending offer in A.47. The caller changes Bob’s sequence while supplying the old unlocking script.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDUx",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "bWFuZGFsYS1zd2FwLTAwMDE=",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "483045022100f3e0efe667230ecd9f750e5c1c3348ee89dadd25cff11ad70ed90a0e131d7ab402200119a0d91d3d766a29fbc23f1ac07a20d6fb01f05306ec1870f59f79256d403c4121035426266ad18afddbd6997b724b619249f334d259430e9733b8fa92f8a6153ecb",
        "sequenceNumber": 4294967294
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDUx",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Completion changes previously committed transaction fields",
  "code": 6
}
```

The existing ALL|FORKID authorization and signatures do not cover this change. The wallet rejects this completion. A separately revalidated action with consent and new signatures may use a different sequence; an old reference does not waive those requirements.

```json
  "status": "success",
```

```json
    "signature": [48, 69, 2, 33, 0, 184, 185, 25, 63, 188, 190, 5, 39, 151, 185, 205, 156, 85, 204, 244, 164, 155, 152, 107, 150, 132, 223, 147, 183, 220, 72, 212, 218, 130, 106, 177, 0, 2, 32, 40, 215, 180, 97, 196, 149, 126, 216, 79, 222, 70, 145, 214, 115, 215, 250, 188, 128, 70, 182, 20, 59, 87, 119, 70, 231, 163, 210, 77, 229, 62, 99]

```

The wallet computes the consensus preimage itself, obtains consent covering the actual commitment, atomically reserves the input and returns DER low-S signature bytes without a sighash byte. The application appends the requested flag when constructing the unlocking script. A different consensus-valid signature nonce may produce different DER bytes; the digest and successful verification are the conformance criteria. Revalidation on later wallet completion cannot constrain an external party holding this signature.

### A.58. Verify against the transaction digest

The signature from A.52 is checked using the same derivation tuple and the actual transaction digest.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json

  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDU4",
```

The envelope’s JSON bytes are not the signed message. Verification does not itself authorize spending or reserve an input. Appending a script sighash byte to the DER-only API signature would also change the API input and is not required here.

### A.61. Preserve exact composable positions

Alice and Bob require input positions 0 and 1, the three requested outputs in their given order, and generated ordinary change at output 3. All fee funding is already accounted for.

### A.66. Duplicate JSON members

The data bytes contain two version members. The duplication is malformed even though the values agree.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDY2",
  "call": "createSignature",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "data": [123, 34, 118, 101, 114, 115, 105, 111, 110, 34, 58, 49, 44, 34, 118, 101, 114, 115, 105, 111, 110, 34, 58, 49, 125],
    "seekPermission": true
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDY2",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Duplicate member in Colored Tokens metadata",
  "code": 6
}
```

The parser detects duplication before treating the JSON object as a map. The same rule applies at every nested level, including remittances, operations and constraints. Missing required members also prevent signing.

### A.67. Caller-supplied service URL is not an envelope field

The otherwise complete signature envelope adds an unknown lookupURL member. The wallet derives A’s service and uses its own configured providers.

### A.68. Reject invalid byte-array elements

The tx member includes 256, which is not a byte.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json

  "args": {
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDY4",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Invalid transaction byte array",
```

Strings, base64, numeric-key objects, booleans, fractions and empty tx arrays likewise cannot stand in for the required nonempty byte array. Bounds and encoding are checked before parsing or allocation; no truncation or modulo conversion is permitted.

### A.69. Reject malformed UTF-8

The data bytes are not valid UTF-8 and therefore cannot encode a request envelope.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDY5",
  "call": "createSignature",
  "args": {
    "protocolID": [
      2,
      "p color issue 41513d3d 41673d3d"
    ],
    "keyID": "color",
    "counterparty": "anyone",
    "data": [195, 40],
    "seekPermission": true
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDY5",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Colored Tokens envelope is not strict UTF-8",
  "code": 6
}
```

No replacement-character decoding is allowed. Plan hex must likewise be lowercase, even-length and complete, with 200 hex characters in every nonfinal chunk.

### A.70. Reject count overflow before allocation

The declared plan part count exceeds uint32. The wallet must not allocate based on this untrusted count.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDcw",
  "call": "createAction",
  "args": {
    "description": "Pay 300 token units to Bob",
    "outputs": [
      {
        "lockingScript": "2004aab38d6e77e0bae3101b8d6c683af6373e9f167cad55d784446013165b44e5022c016d76a914981d7a3bcc51208c145c27145fcd722bcf78c98b88ac",
        "satoshis": 1,
        "outputDescription": "Bob receives 300 units"
      }
    ],
    "labels": [
      "p color plan 0 4294967296 aa"
    ],
    "version": 1,
    "lockTime": 0,
    "options": {
      "randomizeOutputs": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDcw",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Colored Tokens plan count is outside uint32 bounds",
  "code": 6
}
```

No signature is released and provisional reservations are rolled back. No token credit or settled debit is recorded.

The returned transaction ID and ordinary change outpoint identify a locally completed action, not an admitted token receipt. No token topic is submitted yet. ### A.73. Send a previously prepared action

The transfer from A.72 is retained by the wallet and has not conflicted. This request uses the ordinary batch-only createAction flow.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDcz",
  "call": "createAction",
  "args": {
    "description": "Send prepared Colored Tokens transfer",
    "options": {
      "sendWith": [
        "b47d158600304268e860840a766c551699bf002de2d1760e11fc524d83f91fc6"
      ],
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDcz",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "sendWithResults": [
      {
        "txid": "b47d158600304268e860840a766c551699bf002de2d1760e11fc524d83f91fc6",
        "status": "sending"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The wallet resolves the saved plan and authorization, rechecks current token state, and processes the action on the relevant token topic along with Bitcoin submission. The result uses the existing sendWithResults shape; sending does not itself claim mining or topic admission. The wallet records subsequent admission before crediting change. It neither requires invented new inputs/outputs nor charges the same authorized transfer again.

### A.74. Abort after releasing a signature

Start with the pending offer from A.47, including Alice’s already released signature. The wallet cancels its own pending processing.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDc0",
  "call": "abortAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "bWFuZGFsYS1zd2FwLTAwMDE="
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDc0",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "aborted": true
  }
}
```

Cancellation does not retract Alice’s signature. The wallet retains the exposure record and does not immediately offer A to another conflicting action. It can free that exposure after an authenticated completion or conflict makes the capability unusable, or after explicit consent to a conflicting spend that invalidates the old offer. BRC-177 expiry likewise cannot revoke bytes another party holds.

### A.75. Concurrent action cannot reuse reserved input

The pending offer from A.47 already reserves Alice’s only A input. A second application asks for the normal 300-unit payment, without authorizing a conflict with that offer.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDc1",
  "call": "createAction",
  "args": {
    "description": "Pay 300 token units to Bob",
```

### A.90. Action-label queries retain their matching meaning

No saved action has the selected plan label in this isolated read fixture. The wallet authorizes this origin to query its actions.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDkw",
  "call": "listActions",
  "args": {
    "labels": [
      "p color plan 0 5 7b2276657273696f6e223a312c22617373657473223a5b22346631306265393630356630646661636362373339646664346162383936376332636262366236383139346665393936616134313064316533646366373135385f30225d2c22696e70757454"
    ],
    "labelQueryMode": "all",
    "limit": 10,
    "offset": 0
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMDkw",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalActions": 0,
    "actions": []
  }
}
```

The label is an ordinary exact query term at this boundary, not an instruction to reconstruct and execute a new plan. A wallet applies module-aware read permissions and returns the usual action list shape.

### A.94. Direct signature for a committed partial swap

The two named current inputs and all promised outputs are committed by ALL|FORKID; Bob has not supplied his unlocking script. ### A.96. Conditional consideration with a verified remedy

The proposed layout is the same swap, but Alice's wallet has independently verified an applicable asset-ecosystem remedy under its owner-configured policy. That remedy covers the loss of her A contribution if the unbound B source does not supply admitted consideration. The fixture deliberately does not instantiate or standardize the remedy. Alice consents to the precisely described conditional capability, including its limits; disclosure alone would not be enough to authorize it.

The wallet fails explicitly before large allocation or signing; it does not truncate the transaction, omit evidence, or partially decode a plan. The module’s 16 MiB ceiling still applies when the host supports larger requests. Lower plan and proof resource limits must likewise be explicit failures, never silent omission.

### A.102. Bitcoin processing succeeds but an overlay fails

Alice authorizes the ordinary transfer. Bitcoin processing succeeds with the shown transfer transaction ID, but the A-topic submission fails.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTAy",
  "call": "createAction",
  "args": {
    "description": "Pay 300 token units to Bob",
    "outputs": [
      {
        "lockingScript": "2004aab38d6e77e0bae3101b8d6c683af6373e9f167cad55d784446013165b44e5022c016d76a914981d7a3bcc51208c145c27145fcd722bcf78c98b88ac",
        "satoshis": 1,
        "outputDescription": "Bob receives 300 units"
      }
    ],
    "labels": [
      "p color plan 0 5 7b2276657273696f6e223a312c22617373657473223a5b22653534343562313631333630343438346437353561643763313639663365333766363361363836633864316231306533626165303737366538646233616130345f30225d2c226f776e65644f",
      "p color plan 1 5 757470757473223a5b5d2c226f7065726174696f6e73223a5b7b2261737365744964223a22653534343562313631333630343438346437353561643763313639663365333766363361363836633864316231306533626165303737366538646233616130",
      "p color plan 2 5 345f30222c226f7065726174696f6e223a227472616e73666572227d5d2c22696e707574506f736974696f6e73223a5b5d2c226368616e6765506f736974696f6e73223a5b7b2261737365744964223a2265353434356231363133363034343834643735",
      "p color plan 3 5 3561643763313639663365333766363361363836633864316231306533626165303737366538646233616130345f30222c22696e646578223a317d2c7b2261737365744964223a6e756c6c2c22696e646578223a327d5d2c227369676861736854797065",
      "p color plan 4 5 73223a5b5d7d"
    ],
    "version": 1,
    "lockTime": 0,
    "options": {
      "randomizeOutputs": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTAy",
  "status": "error",
  "description": "Transaction completed but Colored Tokens overlay admission is pending",
  "code": 1
}
```

The wallet retains the real completed transaction b47d158600304268e860840a766c551699bf002de2d1760e11fc524d83f91fc6 and reports its unresolved token processing state. It does not pretend Bitcoin completion was rolled back or mark the 700-unit change spendable. A later admission retry reuses this action and does not create a second debit or transaction. Multi-asset processing must similarly retain each topic’s individual result; one successful topic does not authorize credits on the others.

The pending reference remains valid for a later signAction with an empty spends object, because no caller script remains to be supplied. ### A.104. Process a managed-only pending action

Start with the prepared action in A.103. Its sources remain current and the recorded permission remains valid. No new unlocking script is needed.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA0",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "bWFuZGFsYS1wYXktMDAwMDE=",
    "spends": {},
    "options": {
      "returnTXIDOnly": true,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA0",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "txid": "b47d158600304268e860840a766c551699bf002de2d1760e11fc524d83f91fc6"
  }
}
```

The wallet completes the saved action after revalidation and submits it to the A topic. It shares the reservation and authorization already associated with the prepared transaction and does not bill the user’s policy twice.

### A.105. Discover multiple assets without knowing their IDs

This independent inventory contains C reserved by a pending offer, A available, and H available authority. Canonical outpoint ordering puts C before A before H. The origin is authorized to read all three.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA1",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color assets",
    "include": "locking scripts",
    "includeTags": true,
    "limit": 2,
    "offset": 0
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA1",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": 3,
    "outputs": [
      {
        "outpoint": "91fbfbe785fbd6ba33974791c1e59e343a0e783cba0f195bc2b037f1afed7d47.0",
        "satoshis": 1000,
        "lockingScript": "0002b80b6d76a91482a6d141057b6bd1d85839bd83b58f6e657c0a9088ac",
        "spendable": false,
        "tags": [
          "color_state_reserved"
        ]
      },
      {
        "outpoint": "e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04.0",
        "satoshis": 1000,
        "lockingScript": "0002e8036d76a9146cbd20c18911529dd4a707cbe7abcd296a097fe488ac",
        "spendable": true,
        "tags": [
          "color_state_available"
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The client derives C and A IDs from the deploy outpoints and scripts. The C record remains visible but is not available for spending. totalOutputs is 3 actual records, not the two records on this page or a sum of their units.

### A.106. Discover an authority-only asset on the next page

The A.105 snapshot has not changed. The client advances by the two records already read.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA2",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color assets",
    "include": "locking scripts",
    "includeTags": true,
    "limit": 2,
    "offset": 2
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA2",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": 3,
    "outputs": [
      {
        "outpoint": "ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6c.0",
        "satoshis": 1000,
        "lockingScript": "00006d76a91475a06c850b20c3c7e7e9d607f2cc64ba885daaed88ac",
        "spendable": true,
        "tags": [
          "color_state_available"
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The H amount is zero because this is authority. The client lists H with one capability even though it has no fungible value holdings. Every available asset can be discovered without a guessed ID or a global storage basket.

### A.107. BRC-112 token-unit balance

Alice has the available A fixture output, carrying 1000 token units and 1000 satoshis. These happen to coincide in this fixture; the sum is determined by token fields.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA3",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04v0",
    "limit": 1,
    "offset": 999
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA3",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": "1000",
    "outputs": []
  }
}
```

The module ignores limit and offset and returns the whole available token balance. It omits BEEF and never returns output entries for a balance query. Read permission does not grant permission to sign.

### A.108. Authority balance is a capability count

The available H authority fixture is in its authority basket.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA4",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6cv0 authority"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA4",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": "1",
    "outputs": []
  }
}
```

The result means one authority, not one token unit or a zero-valued fungible balance. An accessible H value basket with no available value would return zero separately.

### A.109. Reserved value is excluded from available balance

C’s existing value basket contains only the C output reserved in A.105.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA5",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance 91fbfbe785fbd6ba33974791c1e59e343a0e783cba0f195bc2b037f1afed7d47v0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTA5",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": "0",
    "outputs": []
  }
}
```

The inventory still exposes the reserved record to an authorized reader, but an available-balance query excludes it. Failed pre-signature reservations are rolled back; released-signature exposure is retained under the action lifecycle rules.

### A.110. Exact uint64 maximum over JSON

The fixed-supply issuance from A.38 is complete, admitted, unreserved and owned by Alice. Both ends implement the exact module-balance profile.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEw",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance 520f26d99fd56c7720ee96497b17a04d2b7c845eb6bce3b4682eec06bf36a5f1v0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEw",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": "18446744073709551615",
    "outputs": []
  }
}
```

The API and JSON both preserve this decimal string. The binary field is the byte length 20 followed by the UTF-8 bytes of these 20 decimal digits, not a uint64 VarInt.

### A.111. String balance at the JavaScript numeric boundary

In this independent H state, valid authority issuance has produced an available value total of exactly 9007199254740991 units.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEx",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6cv0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEx",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": "9007199254740991",
    "outputs": []
  }
}
```

This boundary has no special wire treatment: small values, this value and the next integer all use canonical decimal strings. Numeric JSON balances are rejected for this P-basket query.

### A.112. Aggregate greater than uint64 succeeds

In this independent H state, two admitted, available value outputs each carry 18446744073709551615 units. Their exact sum is 36893488147419103230.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEy",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6cv0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEy",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "totalOutputs": "36893488147419103230",
    "outputs": []
  }
}
```

Both outputs satisfy the per-output uint64 limit. The wallet sums them exactly and returns the full decimal string. It does not round, wrap, truncate to one output or require an enumeration fallback.

### A.113. Transport cannot preserve the exact balance

The H balance is 9007199254740993 units, but this wallet adapter cannot emit the exact-integer profile.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEz",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6cv0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTEz",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 2,
  "description": "Exact module balance representation is unsupported"
}
```

The adapter returns an ordinary error rather than casting to Number. An older receiver encountering an unsupported exact result must likewise raise a local protocol error; this example does not claim that all deployed older implementations already enforce that rule.

### A.114. Reject reversed balance prefix

The caller places balance outside the reserved module namespace.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTE0",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "balance p color e5445b1613604484d755ad7c169f3e37f63a686c8d1b10e3bae0776e8db3aa04v0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTE0",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 6,
  "description": "Balance prefix must follow the module identifier"
}
```

The wallet does not bypass module dispatch or normal read permission checks. The supported order is p color balance followed by the asset tag.

### A.115. Discovery permission is required

This origin lacks inventory read permission and forbids an interactive request.

The application sends:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTE1",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color assets",
    "include": "locking scripts",
    "includeTags": true,
    "limit": 2,
    "offset": 0,
    "seekPermission": false
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "bWFuZGFsYTAwMTE1",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 1,
  "description": "Colored Tokens inventory read permission is required"
}
```

No output, asset ID or count is disclosed. A generic cryptographic or spending grant does not automatically authorize this inventory scope.

The wallet reconstructs sequences [7, 10], rechecks source/overlay state and the unchanged price, validates Bob’s new script, and regenerates Alice’s signature because her ALL|FORKID digest commits to the revised sequence set. All scripts now pass, but the transaction is still consensus-non-final. noSend returns the exact bytes without processing or credit. Both released revisions share one exposure record. This noSend result is prepared, not processor-accepted; upon a processing request the wallet must enforce acceptance and supersession under its arrangement. Sequence 10 alone has not revoked sequence 9.

### A.121. Reject a stale signature on a sequence update

Start from A.119 at tip 100200. Bob’s script below was signed for sequence 9, but this request changes it to 10.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTIx",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "47304402202fe02afd05fb618ddae3cfef25e3b2019efd0175838f148c12598e983c42022a02201faa124cca6e925c26563041f6bbc4fb90612c69949bb32213840e41289a8542412103a09d03480b1d26f749090acff56042f63e10e82c14bac70158bb82219e61ca81",
        "sequenceNumber": 10
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "noSend": true,
      "returnTXIDOnly": false,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": false
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTIx",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 6,
  "description": "Provided unlocking script is invalid for revised sequence"
}
```

The wallet verifies the supplied script against the revised preimage and rejects it. It does not overwrite Bob’s signature, restore the old sequence silently, or return a complete transaction. The previously released Alice signature remains recorded; no new signature is released.

Alice would receive 6990 satoshis and Bob 3999 ordinary change plus the token carrier. The fee remains 10. Both scripts, the new preimages and the A overlay are checked. The bid stays pending with noSend. The wallet’s processor arrangement must enforce acceptance of this valid higher-sequence replacement when processing is requested; a warning about old bids is not a substitute. Until that step succeeds, noSend does not claim supersession. A highest-bid-wins assertion also needs validated auction bid-ordering rules.

The wallet returns:

The wallet verifies the conflict, does not sign or process this competing revision, and reconciles its shared exposure record against the observed spender. It cannot credit prospective outputs from both revisions or count a sequence increase as evidence that this revision won.

### A.128. Abort does not expire an auction signature

The independently established A.119 offer has been delivered to Bob. The application cancels local processing before lockTime is reached.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTI4",
  "call": "abortAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTI4",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "aborted": true
  }
}
```

The wallet stops its local pending processing and retains the released-signature exposure. This fixture has no processor-accepted revision to cancel. If one existed, cancellation would require coordination and could not be reported as complete from local deletion alone. The earlier transaction may become final after height 100250; lockTime is not an expiry. A remains reserved/unavailable unless a verified conflict or an independently validated condition prevents the earlier capability from consuming it. An explicit conflicting-spend workflow requires consent and does not release this record merely by being requested.

Reconciliation and resubmission of the retained action MUST use the same deployment and be idempotent; they must not select new inputs or create a second issuance. This does not make a repeated createAction request idempotent: its CWI id is only correlation. The application first inspects the retained action through normal action discovery and the pending inventory, rather than blindly issuing a fresh createAction. Inventory may expose the signed pending output with spendable:false, but its available balance is "0". Once accepted per-asset evidence arrives, reconciliation credits it once.

totalOutputs remains a JSON/API number and the existing numeric VarInt bytes fd c4 09 in the binary response. The P-basket decimal-text format does not apply. Ordinary p color assets inventory counts also retain numeric VarInt encoding, even though the basket starts with p.

### A.133. P-basket balance resource exhaustion fails explicitly

This wallet documents a response-size bound. The requested exact aggregate would exceed it; this case assumes individually valid u64 outputs and sufficient input provenance, not an invalid asset.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTMz",
  "call": "listOutputs",
  "args": {
    "basket": "p color balance ece04819257245af3290370225db8e2d3b9541096d8c0fb3a8cd3c09158c7d6cv0"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTMz",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 1,
  "description": "Exact balance exceeds documented response resource limit"
}
```

No success value is returned. Holdings remain tracked, and the wallet does not truncate digits, cap the sum, silently page the balance, emit a number, or switch back to numeric VarInt. The receiver likewise checks the declared byte length before allocation and rejects over-limit or noncanonical text. This implementation bound is distinct from the per-output uint64 consensus-independent token rule.

### A.135. Accept a higher sequence through the coordinated processors

The wallet retains the A.119 saved context at height 100200. Bob previously completed the original revision and the wallet reconciled its processor acceptance as `386568731d8b742983b6d98a9bb20bb504a402db11aee0de81e09e1e2d335d47`, sequence vector [7, 9]. Both named inputs remain unspent; the token source remains currently admitted. This call requests processing of the valid [7, 10] revision, allowing delayed broadcast.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json

  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM1",

        "sequenceNumber": 10
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "noSend": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": false,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": true
    }
```
```json
  }
}
```

The wallet validates the new Bob signature, regenerates Alice’s affected signature and authenticates the required processors’ acceptance of `a0e189f78d599eca8039f64ce6d223d876fa009beb6a2c1c43cc25d110cb1c35` as the latest revision superseding `386568731d8b742983b6d98a9bb20bb504a402db11aee0de81e09e1e2d335d47`. Their evidence binds the fixture network, both ordered outpoints, sequence vector [7, 10], processor identities and the arrangement’s lifetime and obligations. Only after all required acceptance evidence agrees does this success return. It means processor-accepted pending processing under acceptDelayedBroadcast, not mined settlement or spendable overlay credit. The processors must retain the new revision and suppress stale preference; the application has no processor-specific work to perform.

### A.136. A valid revision refused by a processor is an explicit failure

Use exactly A.135’s valid candidate, but a required processor refuses it under a replacement policy that the wallet was responsible for arranging. Another required processor has already accepted it.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM2",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "4830450221009a0694303ff418868004e89c4134e98731f5b64244156e459cbac1a2e99aa37802203d5af5c1afce1498935dd3970d06ff9f24370c7e9d30cf26ad5b5446da6ac0a6412103a09d03480b1d26f749090acff56042f63e10e82c14bac70158bb82219e61ca81",
        "sequenceNumber": 10
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "noSend": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": false,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": true
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM2",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 1,
  "description": "Valid replacement was not accepted by the required processors"
}
```

The wallet reports failure of the coordination obligation, preserves both transmitted revisions, reconciles partial acceptance and arranges recovery. It does not call the transaction invalid, report successful replacement, silently settle the older bid, or delegate processor negotiation to the application. The error does not undo the processor that already accepted the new bytes.

### A.137. Timeout leaves acceptance uncertain

Use A.135’s candidate. The wallet submitted it, but the required acknowledgement is lost. It cannot yet establish whether all required processors accepted the replacement.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM3",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "4830450221009a0694303ff418868004e89c4134e98731f5b64244156e459cbac1a2e99aa37802203d5af5c1afce1498935dd3970d06ff9f24370c7e9d30cf26ad5b5446da6ac0a6412103a09d03480b1d26f749090acff56042f63e10e82c14bac70158bb82219e61ca81",
        "sequenceNumber": 10
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "noSend": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": false,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": true
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM3",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 1,
  "description": "Processor replacement acceptance is unconfirmed"
}
```

The wallet keeps the candidate transaction ID, exact bytes, reservations and transmitted exposure. It queries authenticated state and retries the same transaction idempotently rather than generating another revision or restoring the earlier one. A later acknowledgement must match the exact candidate, family and applicable arrangement; a generic HTTP success cannot finish reconciliation.

### A.138. An older revision cannot be revived after restart

Processors have accepted `400f4a99000d080530654e0c8faa9b9878534b57c94bfebafee6e37c445101aa` with sequence vector [8, 10], as an independently processed version of A.123. After a wallet restart the application supplies the otherwise valid [7, 10] revision from A.135. The wallet restores its reservation history and authenticates the latest processor state before deciding.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM4",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "4830450221009a0694303ff418868004e89c4134e98731f5b64244156e459cbac1a2e99aa37802203d5af5c1afce1498935dd3970d06ff9f24370c7e9d30cf26ad5b5446da6ac0a6412103a09d03480b1d26f749090acff56042f63e10e82c14bac70158bb82219e61ca81",
        "sequenceNumber": 10
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "noSend": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": false,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": true
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM4",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 6,
  "description": "Replacement sequence is older than the accepted family state"
}
```

The request decreases one sequence relative to the latest accepted revision and therefore is not a valid replacement. The wallet rejects before releasing or submitting a new capability. It must not compare against stale [7, 9] state or treat provider migration/restart as resetting the family. The already accepted higher revision remains authoritative for coordinated processing.

### A.139. Identical accepted bytes are an idempotent resubmission

Independently start after all A.135 acceptance checks succeeded. A later delivery retry uses the existing sendWith flow for that exact stored transaction; it does not repeat createAction or ask signAction to create a new revision.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM5",
  "call": "createAction",
  "args": {
    "description": "Resubmit accepted auction revision",
    "options": {
      "sendWith": [
        "a0e189f78d599eca8039f64ce6d223d876fa009beb6a2c1c43cc25d110cb1c35"
      ],
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": true,
      "returnTXIDOnly": true
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTM5",
  "status": "success",
  "result": {
    "sendWithResults": [
      {
        "txid": "a0e189f78d599eca8039f64ce6d223d876fa009beb6a2c1c43cc25d110cb1c35",
        "status": "unproven"
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

The wallet authenticates the current accepted state and resubmits or confirms the same bytes under the existing arrangement. The unproven status has its ordinary BRC-100 meaning; it does not claim mining or overlay credit. There is no new signature, sequence increment, issuance, reservation or spending charge. If a later revision had been accepted, this older transaction could not be resubmitted as preferred.

### A.140. Cancellation requires processor reconciliation

The A.135 non-final revision is processor-accepted and still outstanding. The application now requests cancellation, but the wallet cannot establish cancellation under the processor arrangement.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTQw",
  "call": "abortAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx"
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTQw",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 1,
  "description": "Processor-accepted action cancellation could not be established"
}
```

The wallet may stop its local work, but it cannot report that the coordinated action was successfully cancelled. It retains the processor state and released exposure, informs the application of the failure and continues reconciliation. Neither local reference deletion nor noSend expiry revokes the transaction or the processors’ obligations.

### A.141. An overlay answer cannot stand in for processor acceptance

Use A.135’s valid candidate. The asset overlay reports a prospective record, and a relay returns HTTP success, but the wallet has no authenticated evidence that the required processors accepted and superseded the prior revision.

An application originates this request to the conforming wallet:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": true,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTQx",
  "call": "signAction",
  "args": {
    "reference": "Y29sb3ItYXVjdGlvbi0wMDAx",
    "spends": {
      "1": {
        "unlockingScript": "4830450221009a0694303ff418868004e89c4134e98731f5b64244156e459cbac1a2e99aa37802203d5af5c1afce1498935dd3970d06ff9f24370c7e9d30cf26ad5b5446da6ac0a6412103a09d03480b1d26f749090acff56042f63e10e82c14bac70158bb82219e61ca81",
        "sequenceNumber": 10
      }
    },
    "options": {
      "noSend": false,
      "returnTXIDOnly": false,
      "acceptDelayedBroadcast": true
    }
  }
}
```

The wallet returns:

```json
{
  "type": "CWI",
  "isInvocation": false,
  "id": "Y29sb3ItMDAwMTQx",
  "status": "error",
  "code": 1,
  "description": "Required authenticated replacement acceptance evidence is missing"
}
```

The wallet rejects the acceptance claim and preserves uncertain transmitted exposure. Token validity, topical membership, request delivery and sequence replacement acceptance are distinct facts. The application receives an explicit failure and is not expected to inspect provider internals to discover the missing guarantee.

## Appendix B — Exact transition and safety vectors

These vectors supplement Appendix A. Amounts are integer token units for
one existing asset, with authenticated admitted inputs and individually valid
encoded outputs. All non-token funding, scripts, source proofs and current
topic checks pass unless a row says otherwise. A successful row requires
exactly the listed operation set in its plan and consent to each positive
quantity; an omitted or extra operation fails before signing. An invalid row
fails before signing regardless of declared operations. An empty operation set
does not authorize a token-bearing action without its exact `assets` entry.

### B.1. Mixed-encoding value accounting

`V`, `T`, `E`, `M` and `B` have the definitions in Transaction evidence and
admission. `A` is the number of admitted authority inputs and outputs.
`X/Y/Z` is the resulting `transferred/minted/burned` triple. Each row is a
separate transaction; no value or authority carries between rows. `tr`,
`mi`, `bu` and `at` abbreviate `transfer`, `mint`, `burn` and
`authority-transfer` only within this table.

| Case | V | T | E | M | B | A in→out | X/Y/Z | Required operations or result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| B01 exact binary transfer | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 100 | 0→0 | 100/0/0 | tr |
| B02 implicit binary burn | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 90 | 0→0 | 90/0/10 | tr, bu |
| B03 no-authority binary excess | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 101 | 0→0 | — | Reject; binary outputs lack coverage even though inputs would be destroyed by an invalid transition. |
| B04 legacy plus binary transfer | 100 | 40 | 0 | 0 | 60 | 0→0 | 100/0/0 | tr |
| B05 mixed over-output | 100 | 70 | 0 | 0 | 40 | 0→0 | — | Reject; remaining value covers only 30 binary units. |
| B06 explicit plus implicit burn | 100 | 20 | 10 | 0 | 60 | 0→0 | 80/0/20 | tr, bu |
| B07 explicit burn exhausts remainder | 100 | 20 | 10 | 0 | 80 | 0→0 | — | Reject; binary amount exceeds the remaining 70. |
| B08 authority mints binary only | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 250 | 1→1 | 0/250/0 | mi, at |
| B09 authority with value burn | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 90 | 1→1 | 90/0/10 | tr, bu, at; authority does not erase the burn. |
| B10 authority with exact value coverage | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 100 | 1→1 | 100/0/0 | tr, at; `mint` is extra and fails. |
| B11 authority with binary excess | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 140 | 1→1 | 100/40/0 | tr, mi, at |
| B12 mixed, simultaneous mint and burn | 100 | 40 | 10 | 0 | 70 | 1→1 | 90/20/10 | tr, mi, bu, at; net supply rises 10. |
| B13 mixed, remaining value burned | 100 | 40 | 10 | 0 | 30 | 1→1 | 70/0/30 | tr, bu, at |
| B14 legacy mint plus mixed transfer | 100 | 40 | 10 | 30 | 50 | 1→1 | 90/30/10 | tr, mi, bu, at |
| B15 equal legacy mint and burn | 100 | 0 | 100 | 100 | 0 | 1→0 | 0/100/100 | mi, bu, authority-end; net zero does not cancel either consent. |
| B16 authority cannot fund legacy transfer | 0 | 1 | 0 | 100 | 0 | 1→1 | — | Reject; `T + E > V` despite authority. |
| B17 legacy mint only | 0 | 0 | 0 | 100 | 0 | 1→1 | 0/100/0 | mi, at; no `transfer`. |
| B18 legacy transfer plus binary mint | 100 | 100 | 0 | 0 | 100 | 1→1 | 100/100/0 | tr, mi, at; the same value input cannot fund both outputs. |
| B19 authority-free legacy mint | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0→0 | — | Reject; `mint` requires an admitted authority input. |
| B20 wide aggregate | 36893488147419103230 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 36893488147419103230 | 0→0 | 36893488147419103230/0/0 | tr; two maximum-uint64 inputs and outputs, summed exactly without uint64 overflow. |

For B20 each input and output amount is 18446744073709551615;
the table totals deliberately exceed uint64. A single encoded output above
uint64 remains invalid. If B16's legacy mint is independently admissible under
BRC-161, that does not make its legacy transfer valid: the wallet still rejects
the action because the intended transition is not wholly admitted.

### B.2. Authority and operation-set boundaries

All rows have a single existing asset, no value amounts, valid authority
scripts and separately verified controller consent. The operation is in
addition to any positive value operation from B.1; count changes never turn
authority into numeric value.

| Case | Admitted authority inputs | Authority outputs | Required result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| B21 end one | 1 | 0 | `authority-end` |
| B22 transfer one | 1 | 1 | `authority-transfer`, even if the controller changes |
| B23 split one | 1 | 2 | `authority-split` |
| B24 end several | 2 | 0 | `authority-end` |
| B25 combine | 2 | 1 | `authority-combine` |
| B26 same count | 2 | 2 | `authority-transfer`, including swaps of controllers |
| B27 net split | 2 | 3 | `authority-split` |
| B28 unauthorized creation | 0 | 1 | Reject; no authority input |
| B29 absent capability | 0 | 0 | No authority operation; an asserted one is extra and fails. |

A fixed or authority deploy is instead its new asset's sole deploy operation.
The BRC-162 deploy must occupy output zero. Its genesis amount or authority
is not charged as an existing-asset transfer or mint. A BRC-161 deploy follows
that encoding's own allowed position. A binary prefix takes precedence over
an embedded legacy inscription; its legacy text cannot add a second amount or
operation. Assets are evaluated independently: a surplus of asset A cannot
cover a deficit of asset B.

### B.3. Signature commitment and provider boundaries

The signature rows assume consensus-valid source scripts and otherwise
authorized unilateral disposition. `nIn` and `nOut` are transaction input
and output counts. Both direct signing and managed-action signing MUST produce
the same accept/reject decision for the stated context. A rejection releases
no signature or pending signature capability.

| Case | Flag | Signing input | nIn/nOut | Required result |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| B30 BIP143 SINGLE matched | `0x43` | 0 | 1/1 | May sign; commits output zero after ordinary validation. |
| B31 BIP143 SINGLE absent | `0x43` | 1 | 2/1 | Reject; no corresponding output, even if that digest algorithm uses zero `hashOutputs`. |
| B32 BIP143 SINGLE/ANYONECANPAY absent | `0xc3` | 1 | 2/1 | Reject; ANYONECANPAY does not cure the missing output. |
| B33 Chronicle SINGLE matched | `0x63` | 0 | 1/1 | May sign using the original transaction digest algorithm, not BIP143 fields. |
| B34 Chronicle SINGLE absent | `0x63` | 1 | 2/1 | Reject; the original algorithm's missing-output result is the constant uint256 value 1. |
| B35 Chronicle SINGLE/ANYONECANPAY absent | `0xe3` | 1 | 2/1 | Reject for the same reason; no outpoint or output is committed by the degenerate digest. |
| B36 NONE with no outputs committed | `0x42` | 0 | 1/1 | May sign only if its actual unilateral consequences or a verified safeguard satisfy authorization; the output shown in a subject is not promised. |
| B37 ALL/ANYONECANPAY conditional swap | `0xc1` | 0 | 2/2 | Reject when a named foreign input is required consideration but uncommitted and no independently verified remedy applies. |
| B38 ALL/ANYONECANPAY with remedy | `0xc1` | 0 | 2/2 | May sign only after the wallet verifies an applicable remedy under the asset system's policy; the remedy is outside this BRC. |

For B31–B35 the subject and source may otherwise be valid; missing output
coverage alone triggers rejection. The actual signed message is computed from
the selected consensus algorithm, never from a caller-supplied digest.
SIGHASH_SINGLE's constant-one result is a consensus property, not a permissible
wallet signature vector.

| Case | Evidence or policy condition | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B39 no caller hint | Authenticated token source with no `inputTopics` member | Derive asset, topic and configured provider from the source; continue only if current admission passes. |
| B40 caller topic hint | Otherwise valid plan or signature envelope contains `inputTopics` | Reject as an unknown version-1 member; do not let it select a topic. |
| B41 differing wallet policies | Provider P admits the source; P is trusted by wallet A but wallet B requires other evidence | A may proceed under its configuration; B fails closed until its own configured rule is satisfied. Neither wallet can deem the other's policy authoritative. |
| B42 issuer convention mismatch | Source is admitted by one wallet, but intended counterparty requires a different asset-network convention | Do not promise counterparty acceptance; apply the wallet's asset and counterparty policy before release. |
| B43 provider disagrees or is stale | Required current evidence is insufficient under this wallet's configuration | Reject before signature or broadcast; an old proof cannot substitute for current spendability. |
| B44 delayed actor | A later untrusted participant omits a required signature or changes an uncommitted output | No loss beyond the releasing user's unconditional authorized disposition, or a verified system-specific remedy must cover the conditional loss; otherwise reject the initial release. |
| B45 unrelated controller | Alice releases a valid signature for her input; Bob's separately controlled input remains unsigned | Alice's signature cannot spend Bob's input or invalidate Bob's independently admitted asset. |

These cases set wallet safety and evidence boundaries. Provider choice,
facilitator functions, negotiation, participation authorization, acceptance
conventions and remedies remain choices of the applicable asset ecosystem,
issuer, wallet, application and users, rather than a universal rule imposed by
this BRC.
